# Farmer’s Delight Legacy ecosystem audit and implementation roadmap

Audit date: 2026-10-17. Targets: Forge 1.6.4, 1.7.10, 1.8.9, and 1.12.2.

This records the source audit and gameplay contract. The revised paired-port strategy below supersedes the original target order; implementation lives under `versions/1.12.2` and `versions/1.7.10`. Audit findings and original source ledgers remain reference evidence, not runtime certification. Counts and findings describe the local source snapshots below. Remote-tracking branches were inspected without checking them out or fetching updates; their names do not certify the latest upstream state. No Minecraft client/server, Gradle build, automated gameplay test, or recipe viewer was run. Static resource checks establish file/reference coverage, not runtime acquisition or visual correctness.

Navigation: [Inventory](#b-ecosystem-inventory-before-subsystem-analysis) · [FD architecture](#c-farmers-delight-architecture-map) · [Addons](#d-addon-by-addon-audit-and-generation-comparison) · [Compatibility matrix](#f-legacy-feature-and-content-compatibility-matrix) · [Roadmap/tests](#k-exact-phased-roadmap-and-acceptance-strategy) · [First milestone](#m-recommended-first-coding-milestone) · [Four-target baseline](#o-four-target-beta-baseline-and-registration-contract) · [Source ledgers](#appendices-source-ledgers).

## A. Executive summary

The ecosystem is feasible as native legacy implementations, with explicitly reduced content where vanilla prerequisites do not exist. Keep five independently released mod repositories, with the reused API inside Farmer’s Delight. Do not add a sixth required API mod, merge addons into FD, or backport ocean monuments, the End update, the Nether update, or the Aquatic update merely to unlock recipes.

Treat **1.12.2 and 1.7.10 as co-primary initial ports**. Define each system’s shared gameplay contract, implement it natively on 1.12.2 and immediately on 1.7.10, compare equivalent acceptance results, and resolve discrepancies before proceeding. Neither version is the canonical implementation. After the paired FD core is stable, target 1.8.9, then 1.6.4; the latter intentionally has the most reduced ecosystem. Keep genuinely shared semantic code Java-7-compatible for that eventual target.

Recommended addon order: **Corn → Ocean (after license clarification) → End → Brewin’ and Chewin’**. Corn exercises the broadest reused FD surface with no extra machine. Ocean is smaller but has licensing ambiguity and major acquisition gaps. End needs dedicated stove/teleport/drop work and extensive pre-1.9 gating. Brewin is the most mechanically complex: a keg, temperature conditions, liquid-item inputs, XP, GUI, automation, effects, and cheese aging. If Ocean’s license remains unresolved, skip its implementation slot and continue with End; the other work is independent.

Use the checked-out FD 0.6.0 implementation as the initial content/interaction baseline, and deliberately incorporate applicable later fixes. FD 1.18.2 is a useful intermediate behavioral reference for addons, not a package tree to copy wholesale. FD 1.20/1.21 supply important correctness fixes but also change content and progression; importing their entire design would silently change scope.

Major findings that must shape implementation:

- The clones are **not already legacy ports**. Three checkouts use 1.16.5, Brewin uses 1.18.2, and Ocean uses 1.19.2. The latter two directly import a newer FD package/API generation.
- FD cooking is a six-ingredient, nine-slot machine with an internal meal buffer, serving containers, XP history, heat predicates, sided automation, and comparator output. Cutting is an inventory-backed world interaction with chance outputs and dispenser support, not a crafting-table recipe.
- Corn’s ten Java files inherit important behavior from **MMLib**. Its FD dependency lower bound does not prevent references to configuration fields absent from the local FD checkout.
- Brewin’s common mixin list includes a client renderer target. The keg automation handler cannot insert into its liquid slot. Its normal temperature category bypasses the temperature range. These are concrete source properties to resolve deliberately, not behavior to duplicate blindly.
- End’s five loot-modifier JSON files lack a `type` selector, and three recipes use an undeclared external `farmersrespite:brewing` serializer. Their existence does not prove those acquisition paths work.
- Ocean’s `mods.toml` claims MIT, but `LICENSE.txt` is the Forge MDK’s LGPL text. Its FD dependency is under the wrong owner table with a malformed-looking range. Treat redistribution rights as unresolved until clarified; do not assume either label licenses all addon assets/code.
- All four targets lack sea pickles, seagrass, kelp, honey bottles, sweet berries, Netherite, and the 1.16 Nether vegetation. Guardians exist on 1.8.9/1.12.2; chorus/shulkers/levitation and the dragon-breath collection loop exist only on 1.12.2 among these targets. Pufferfish/salmon are item metadata variants on 1.7.10 onward; their mob entities are unnecessary for the recipes.

The deliverable includes exhaustive checked-out registry, recipe, tag-definition, loot, food-definition, and direct FD-import ledgers in the appendices. Those ledgers preserve exact source IDs and data for implementation handoff; the narrative identifies authoritative code and decisions rather than treating every textual reference as a proven gameplay path.

## C. Farmer’s Delight architecture map

Source paths in this section are beneath `FarmersDelight-Legacy/src/main/java/vectorwing/farmersdelight/` unless stated otherwise.

### C.1 Lifecycle, registration, and authority

`FarmersDelight.java` registers items, blocks, effects, enchantments, particles, sounds, entity/tile/container types, recipe serializers, and common/client configs. `setup/CommonEventHandler.java` owns setup, compostables, dispenser behavior, biome generation, trades, and extra food effects. `setup/ClientEventHandler.java` is a client-only subscriber; the checked-out history already includes a fix preventing server execution of client setup. Preserve that fix.

`registry/Mod*` classes own registry identity. `crafting` owns serialized recipes, while `data` produces their resource representation. `tile` and `blocks` own gameplay mutations. `integration` is optional viewer/scripting glue. `loot`, `world`, and `setup` extend vanilla acquisition. There is no general custom packet channel in the five checked-out mods: native container, block-entity, recipe synchronization, and Forge entity spawn mechanisms do most of the work.

Legacy code should retain those responsibilities with native lifecycle hooks. Do not make recipes, a viewer cache, or client tile state authoritative. All inventory consumption, growth/harvest drops, serving, damage/effects, and acquisition rules execute on the owning world thread.

### C.2 Cooking Pot: exact machine contract

Authoritative path: `blocks/CookingPotBlock` → `tile/CookingPotTileEntity` → `crafting/CookingPotRecipe`. UI path: `tile/container/CookingPotContainer`, `CookingPotMealSlot`, `CookingPotResultSlot` → `client/gui/CookingPotScreen`. Automation: `tile/inventory/CookingPotItemHandler`. Shared heat: `tile/IHeatableTileEntity`. Sync base: `tile/FDSyncedTileEntity`.

| Concern | Observed behavior to preserve or deliberately correct |
|---|---|
| Slots | Inputs 0–5; internal meal display/buffer 6; serving-container input 7; finished output 8. Ingredient count is the number of occupied input slots, not a sum of stack sizes. |
| Matching | Shapeless bipartite ingredient matching via Forge RecipeMatcher; one stack cannot satisfy two ingredient positions. Exactly the recipe’s number of input slots must be occupied. Duplicate egg/tomato requirements remain duplicate positions. |
| Progress | Heated + valid inputs + compatible buffer advances cook time; invalid recipe resets; unheated progress decays by 2 toward zero. Recipes default to 200 ticks; exact resource values remain authoritative. |
| Heat | Source immediately below; if allowed, one conductor block between pot and source. A source’s lit property must be true when present. Sources include FD stove, magma, and tray sources; conductors include hopper and optional Create chute. No fuel inventory or FE energy. |
| Batch completion | Add the recipe output count to meal buffer; consume one from each occupied ingredient slot; eject ingredient container remainders beside the pot; record one recipe execution for XP. Serving containers are a separate accounting step. |
| Containers | Explicit recipe container wins; otherwise output’s container item. Containerless meals move straight to output. A matching bowl/bottle/custom container converts one buffer portion into one output portion. Right-click serving also consumes a held container. |
| Automation | Up/null view inserts **and extracts** inputs 0–5. Other sides insert only into 7 and extract only from 8. Slot 6 cannot be extracted through that handler. Do not implement a generic hopper inventory that exposes the buffer. |
| Redstone | `CookingPotBlock.getAnalogOutputSignal` delegates to FD’s item-handler fullness calculation. Preserve its slot set/formula, not just output fullness. |
| XP | Recipe-use counts persist in `RecipesUsed`; player result-slot extraction pays/clears stored XP; block removal grants stored XP. Hopper extraction does not emulate a player payout. Preserve fractional XP rounding and avoid duplicate collection after reload/break. |
| Persistence | `Inventory`, `CookTime`, `CookTimeTotal`, `Container`, optional `CustomName`, `RecipesUsed`. A harvested pot can carry the meal buffer through its loot-copy function; unrelated ingredient slots drop separately. |
| Sync | Native tile update packet/tag for inventory/container data; two GUI progress integers; native recipe serializer network methods. Legacy ports need their own server-to-client recipe snapshot for custom types. |
| Lookup | Last-recipe ID + input-change flag avoids unconditional full searching. Cached recipe identity must be invalidated when recipe definitions change. |

**Corrections to carry forward:** 0.6 compares meal/output identity too weakly for NBT-sensitive recipes and can retain progress across recipe swaps. Later FD uses item+tag/component equality, resets progress appropriately, and supplies remainder overrides for input foods that do not advertise a container. Use exact item/damage/NBT matching in legacy buffers and test recipe swaps. Do not copy the modern component system into legacy versions.

There is no native pot recipe book in this checkout. JEI displays cooking, supplies catalysts and a click region, and transfers six ingredients. Later FD’s recipe book is a separate feature; it is not required to reproduce the checked-out UI. Keep recipe-viewer availability in the first machine milestone.

### C.3 Cutting Board, knives, tools, and secondary outputs

Path: `blocks/CuttingBoardBlock` → `tile/CuttingBoardTileEntity.processStoredItemUsingTool` → `crafting/CuttingBoardRecipe` → `crafting/ingredients/ChanceResult`. One item is placed on the board; processing verifies both input and tool; results are spawned with a directional offset, the tool takes one durability operation, and the stored input is removed. Invalid item/tool produces the corresponding message. A tool can also be displayed embedded in the board; that flag persists as `IsItemCarved` alongside inventory.

The tile exposes a one-slot, slot-limit-one item handler, allowing hopper-style insertion/extraction. No board GUI exists. Dispensers can apply registered tools through `tile/dispenser/CuttingBoardDispenseBehavior`; the wrapper saves/delegates the pre-existing dispenser behavior when not facing a board. Preserve this fallback instead of breaking vanilla shears/flint/tool behavior. Later FD intercepts the dispenser centrally, broadening compatible-tool coverage; adapt that behavior only if a qualified hook is necessary on the target.

Cutting permits up to four distinct output stacks. `ChanceResult` rolls each unit within a stack independently against `chance + configuredFortuneBonus × FortuneLevel`; it does not perform one all-or-nothing roll for the whole stack. The configurable bonus defaults to 0.1 per level. Carry NBT, counts, probability, tool predicates, and custom sound IDs through recipes and viewers. Ordinary knives reject Fortune at the enchanting table, but other compatible tools/commands may still supply Fortune; the recipe mechanism must retain it.

`items/KnifeItem` provides a custom knife tool type plus FD/Forge knife tags, tool-tier damage/mining/durability, compatible enchantments, pumpkin carving, and vanilla cake slicing. `enchantments/BackstabbingEnchantment` uses a horizontal direction dot product threshold `< -0.5` behind the target, triggered through LivingHurtEvent. Damage is server gameplay; renderer/camera direction must not become authoritative. Source knockback detection checks actual KnifeItem, while slicing/drop recognition uses tags; the proposed explicit knife service should reconcile compatible-tool recognition without treating every sword as a knife.

Pre-1.9 lacks offhand and attack-speed mechanics. Preserve primary-hand board placement/cutting and legacy combat semantics; do not introduce cooldown combat merely to retain a modern constructor parameter. Pumpkin carving has no distinct uncarved-pumpkin block on these targets: gate that modern transformation rather than minting duplicate pumpkins for seed farming.

### C.4 Stove, skillet, and utility tools

`tile/StoveTileEntity` has six one-item cooking positions with independent times and cached recipe IDs. It uses campfire cooking recipes, requires the stove lit and its grilling area unobstructed, and emits cooked items into the world. Stove is a reusable heat source without a fuel-storage simulation. Lighting, extinguishing, collision damage, smoke/steam, grille item placement, facing, and obstruction are all part of behavior.

`tile/SkilletTileEntity`, `blocks/SkilletBlock`, and `items/SkilletItem` reuse campfire recipes. A placed skillet stores a stack and cooks/emits one unit at a time; losing heat decays progress. The skillet item carries its identity/enchantments and handheld cooking NBT; Fire Aspect changes cooking time. Handheld use draws an ingredient from the other hand near a heat source or while on fire. On 1.6.4/1.7.10/1.8.9 this interaction requires a native replacement, while placed cooking remains fully implementable. Recommended legacy interaction: load a selected ingredient through a small inventory selection interaction while holding the skillet, then use it near heat; explicitly document this control difference before implementation. Do not make a mandatory offhand mod dependency.

`items/RopeItem`, `blocks/RopeBlock`, and `SafetyNetBlock` provide placement/extension, pane-style connections, climbing/utility behavior, and fall protection. Bell tie state and waterlogging are modern attachments, not reasons to omit the rope itself. Rope/item fuel values, feed items, and rotten tomatoes must not be lost because they are outside the two main machines. `DogFoodItem` and `HorseFeedItem` use entity membership predicates; modern mount types are gated while native wolves/horses retain their effects. `RottenTomatoEntity` has item projectile rendering, impact effects/advancement behavior, and Forge spawn packets; reimplement with native tracking/spawn support.

### C.5 Food, effects, bowls, drinks, feasts, and leftovers

`items/Foods.java` owns nutrition, saturation modifier, fast/always-eat/meat flags, and vanilla/FD effects. Constants are **600, 1200, 3600, 6000 ticks** for brief/short/medium/long. The medium value is 3 minutes even where comments/config descriptions mention another duration. The appendix records each food builder rather than inferring values from names.

`items/ConsumableItem.finishUsingItem` applies custom effects server-side, consumes food or a nonfood consumable, awards native statistics/criteria, and returns the container to the hand, inventory, or ground if needed. Preserve creative handling, stacked meals, full inventory, bowls/bottles/custom shells/tankards, and partial stacks. `DrinkItem` uses a 32-tick drinking animation; milk bottle removes one random milk-curable effect, hot cocoa one harmful milk-curable effect, melon juice heals 2 health, and popsicles extinguish fire. These are not equivalent to calling vanilla milk-bucket consumption.

`effects/ComfortEffect` heals 1 health every 80 ticks when hurt, unless Regeneration is active or a player has positive saturation. `NourishmentEffect` removes 0.1 exhaustion per tick above its threshold, except while eligible for saturated natural regeneration. The latter reads exhaustion through the access transformer. Pre-1.9 does not have the same saturated-regeneration branch, so implement the same intent against that target’s FoodStats behavior rather than adding newer healing wholesale. The vanilla-soup Comfort event uses the actual food mapping/tag; use source values, not tooltip prose. Client overlays are visual-only.

`blocks/FeastBlock` has horizontal facing, servings 0–4, a supplied serving item, optional leftovers, container validation, support checks, and comparator = remaining servings. Roast chicken, shepherd’s pie, ham and stuffed pumpkin customize shape/leftover behavior. At zero, a leftover plate can remain and be broken for its loot. `PieBlock` uses facing + bites 0–3, direct eating or knife slicing, exactly four portions, and comparator = remaining bites. These simple states do not justify a tile entity.

Vanilla soup stacking is handled by `mixin/SoupItemMixin` and config allow/deny lists. Its container-return interception and stack limit are separate obligations; enabling stacking without correct finish-use behavior loses bowls. On targets requiring a global hook, qualify the Mixin environment first. FD’s own stackable meals do not depend on globally transforming vanilla soups.

### C.6 Farming and seed acquisition

Core classes: `CabbagesBlock`, `OnionsBlock`, `TomatoesBlock`, `RiceCropBlock`, `RiceUpperCropBlock`, wild-crop subclasses, `RichSoilBlock`, `RichSoilFarmlandBlock`, `OrganicCompostBlock`, `MushroomColonyBlock`; world placement in `world/CropPatchGeneration`, `world/features/RiceCropFeature`, and the biome event.

- Cabbage/tomato have seeds; onion is itself plantable; rice grains plant the lower crop. Wild plants, native crop drops, chest loot, and configured trades form acquisition paths. Shears preserve wild plants. Do not replace these paths with arbitrary grass seed drops.
- Tomatoes use ages 0–7 in this source. Right-click mature harvest yields 1–2 tomatoes, a 5% rotten-tomato chance, and reduces age by two. Later rope-grown tomato vines are a real feature change, not just a renamed CropBlock.
- Rice requires source water at the lower crop and suitable ground. Lower and upper ages are 0–3; upper growth needs free space. Bonemeal can span the two parts. Legacy fluid coexistence requires an explicit native rice block with water-like semantics and restoration rules; generic waterlogging does not exist on any requested target.
- Rich soil may boost compatible growables at configurable chance (0.2 default), forms mushroom colonies under light ≤12, and respects exclusion membership. Rich farmland hydrates and preserves rich soil rather than reverting to ordinary dirt; it is immune to trampling. Preserve growth-event cancellation where available and loaded-chunk bounds.
- Organic compost uses random ticks, stages 0–7, nearby activators, water and lighting to become rich soil; comparator behavior is stage-dependent. It is not a timed tile machine.
- Mushroom colonies have four visual/growth stages, light/soil requirements and shears harvesting. Normal bonemeal-target checks return false even though a growth method exists; do not accidentally allow direct bonemeal just because rich soil invokes growth.
- Wild generation uses climate/category predicates: beach cabbages/beetroots, swamp/jungle rice, hot tomatoes, temperate carrots/onions, cool potatoes. Village compost heaps use jigsaw structures, which need native old village-generation integration or an explicitly documented omission; chest injection alone is not the same feature.

### C.7 Storage and decoration

The checked-out name is **pantry**, not cabinet. Eight wood pantries use a 27-slot lockable/loot inventory with open/close state/sounds and fullness comparator. Crates/bags/bales are compression/decompression blocks, not inventories. Basket is a **27-slot directional collector** with hopper-like pulling and redstone disable behavior; do not reduce it to a decorative chest or assume it has a vanilla hopper’s five slots.

Canvas signs have standing/wall blocks for undyed plus 16 colors, sign text, rendering materials, coloring/text defaults, native sign editing/sync, and tile data. Rugs, tatami variants, safety nets and ropes use models and block state. Modern hanging signs and cabinet renames are later additions, not present baseline requirements. Pre-1.7 acacia/dark oak and all-target crimson/warped decorations require optional matching wood providers or gating; do not relabel oak as those species.

### C.8 Loot, world integration, configs, and hooks

`loot/modifiers/AddItemModifier`, `AddLootTableModifier`, and `PastrySlicingModifier`, `setup/LootModifierHandler`, global-loot configuration and the individual modifier resources jointly own extra drops. Source includes knife scavenging (pig/hoglin ham, feathers, leather, rabbit hide, shulker shell, string), straw from grass/mature crops, pastry slicing, and chest/village injection. Copy-meal/copy-skillet functions preserve carried machine contents. `SmokerCookFunction` converts suitable dropped meat through smoker recipes. No custom fishing acquisition path was found; do not invent one.

`world/VillageStructures` adds compost heaps; event code changes farmer and wandering-trader offers. There is no wandering trader on any requested target, nor modern village-house chest families on the oldest versions. Native farmer offers can retain crop exchange intent; chest loot uses only genuinely equivalent existing structures, with non-equivalent omissions recorded.

Common config includes crate recipes, farmer/wanderer offers, rich-soil chance, cutting Fortune, soup stacking/filter semantics, chest/compost/wild generation and rarity. Client config includes overlays, tooltip effects and dark-sign backgrounds. Reimplement the native Configuration/GUI equivalent and authoritative server recipe conditions; do not let a client’s crate setting silently disagree with the server.

Configured mixins/accessors: campfire bale smoke height; rich-soil tree replacement protection; vanilla soup behavior; chicken/pig breeding ingredients; recipe-map accessor; canvas sign material; rug breaking texture. The AT exposes exhaustion, dispenser behavior, loot-function registration, and a jigsaw constructor. The legacy recipe registry eliminates the recipe-map accessor. Legacy-owned signs/renderers remove the need to inject modern sign rendering. No deep hook is justified for absent campfires/jigsaws. Preserve required behavior through native events/owned implementations before deciding which remaining hooks need Mixins.

## D. Addon-by-addon audit and generation comparison

### D.1 Corn Delight

**Difficulty:** moderate, despite its small source count. Packages are `block`, `item`, `worldgen`, `client`, plus lifecycle/config/tag helpers. Dependency graph: Corn → FD; Corn → MMLib in source; optional resource recipes → Create / Immersive Engineering. No addon-to-addon cycle found.

Content is wild corn, tall corn crop, corn crate, kernel bag, plantable seeds, raw/grilled/boiled corn, popcorn, creamed corn, soup, creamy drink, batter/bread, raw/cooked tortilla, taco and stuffing. The registry ledger lists all 17 item IDs. It has six cooking recipes and two cutting recipes; crafting, furnace, smoker/campfire, and machine integrations complete the chain.

FD reuse: creative tab; `WildCropsBlock`; food-duration constants; Comfort/Nourishment effects; cooking/cutting serializers; wild-crop membership and ingredient tags. `CornCommonEvent` reads `Configuration.CROPS_ON_SHIPWRECKS` and `CROPS_ON_VILLAGE_HOUSES`, neither present in the audited FD 0.6.0 config. This confirms source/API drift despite the declared minimum. Replace this direct implementation dependency with a stable FD loot-configuration policy/API, preserving toggles rather than fabricating fields.

Custom tall crop inherits **MMLib HighCropBlock**; upper part appears at age 4, and local shapes cover eight ages. Food uses MMLib `ItemFoodBase`, `ItemFoodSeeds`, and `FoodInfo` with hunger/saturation, water, nutrients, decay, heat capacity, cooking temperature and effects. The exact pinned MMLib implementation is not cloned and could not be retrieved by the bounded web lookup. Consequently, upper-half break/drop delegation, nutrition-library behavior and inherited return/drink semantics remain a **specific dependency audit gap**, not a claim of confirmed parity. Obtain that exact dependency source/JAR before implementing Corn’s crop/food replacement. Do not copy unlicensed MMLib code; the available upstream page did not establish a license grant.

Recommend native Corn crop/food classes instead of porting the whole nutrition library. Keep Corn-specific behavior in Corn. Share only already demonstrated FD consumable/ingredient behavior. Do not add a generic high-crop framework to FD solely for this one crop. Preserve source food data as reference; supplemental nutrition/decay integration is optional only if its external ecosystem exists.

Later source inspected: upstream `main` (1.18.2, 1.0.6), `1.20.x` (1.20.1, 1.2.10), `1.21` (1.21.1). 1.18.2 adds tortilla chips, caramel popcorn, corn dogs, classic corn dogs, nachos bowl/block and popcorn box. It changes stuffing hunger **18 → 12** and makes the creamy corn drink use a drink-specific implementation. Prefer those two gameplay corrections, retain the extra content as an explicit extension tranche after the baseline. 1.20 adds corncob, cob pipe, corn popsicle, village/trade integration and optional Curios code; 1.21 does not contain every 1.20 addition. A newer number is not a superset. The 1.20 history also fixes FD 1.3 registry-field incompatibility. Do not reproduce that brittle field coupling in the legacy API.

Native 1.16.5 is the simpler starting reference, but use the drink correction and 12-hunger stuffing baseline. The 1.18+ datagen architecture is build convenience. Retain generated recipes/models as behavioral data; do not transplant it to 1.6.4.

Legacy blockers: the sweet-berry cornbread-stuffing recipe and Corn’s alternative stuffed-pumpkin recipe are unavailable on all four targets; compost-bin registration has no vanilla composter; shipwreck/modern village loot has no equivalent structure. Gate these specific paths; ordinary corn acquisition through wild generation survives. Native high crops fit age 0–7 plus upper-bit metadata without a tile entity, provided double-drop prevention is implemented.

### D.2 Ocean’s Delight

**Difficulty:** low for the squid/guardian subset; moderate for faithful acquisition, serving and compatibility. Packages are registries/setup, foods, one feast block, datagen and layer registration. Dependency graph: Ocean → FD; optional data → Diet. JEI is a build dependency but no custom local viewer category exists; FD’s categories discover its recipes.

The 22 registered items cover squid tentacles/cuts/rings/sticks, guardian/tail/soup, elder slabs/slices/rolls/wraps, fugu slices/rolls, braised sea pickle, stuffed cod, honey-fried kelp and seagrass salad. One non-tile feast block serves bowl-of-guardian-soup portions. No custom entity, worldgen, capability, config, packet or animation library is implemented.

Direct FD reuse: `common.block.FeastBlock`; Comfort; `ModItems` cooked rice/rope; `ForgeTags` dough/crops/eggs/cabbage; cooking/cutting recipe builders; `farmersdelight:add_item` loot serializer. JSON modifiers are stored in **FD’s namespace** but produce Ocean’s items; preserve their registration ownership carefully and avoid collisions. Guardian drop is unconditional for ordinary guardians; elder slab and squid tentacles require a killer holding an FD knife. Cutting yields tentacles → 3 cuts; pufferfish → 6 fugu slices + bonemeal; elder slab → 9 slices + bonemeal; guardian → tail + bonemeal.

The soup recipe outputs the **placeable soup block**, with a bowl as a pot output container; servings then produce bowl-of-soup items with their own return behavior. Do not flatten these two stages into one food output. Several foods use plain Item rather than FD ConsumableItem despite declaring bowl remainders; verify meal eating returns before carrying that source behavior forward.

All four legacy targets lack aquatic plants/honey. Gate braised sea pickle, seagrass salad, honey-fried kelp and the kelp-dependent stuffed-cod chain unless an explicitly supported optional provider supplies the real prerequisites. Squid works on all four; fugu on 1.7.10+ via exact fish metadata; guardians/elders on 1.8.9/1.12.2. On those targets elders may be a subtype rather than a distinct entity class; use a native entity predicate, not a hardcoded modern registry name.

Later/earlier comparison: local upstream 1.18.2 has the same 16-class scale but lacks four 1.19 items: stuffed cod, cooked stuffed cod, honey-fried kelp and seagrass salad. Upstream 1.20 has equivalent registered content plus creative-tab restructuring. 1.21 NeoForge adds stuffed squid, cooked stuffed squid and kelp-encrusted cod. Use 1.18.2 as a simpler implementation reference, **retain the chosen 1.19 content in the gating ledger**, and record 1.21 additions as an extension, not silently lost content. Do not transplant Fabric/NeoForge loaders.

Before any redistribution, resolve the metadata/license-file conflict with the rights holder or an authoritative applicable grant. No code/assets were copied by this audit. If unresolved, exclude Ocean from shipping; clean-room concepts would be a separate, deliberately scoped project.

### D.3 End’s Delight

**Difficulty:** high on 1.12.2, very high/reduced on the older targets. Packages include block/item/food/material registries, stove tile/renderer, loot serializers and succulent generation. Dependency graph: End → FD; three unguarded brewing resources → **Farmer’s Respite**, not Brewin. Create fan-heater tag is optional data. No Respite repository is present, so its machine is outside the audited implementation scope.

Content includes four knives (end stone, purpur, tooth, egg shell), pearl/chorus grains, succulent and dried flower, dragon meat/leg/tooth, non-hatchable egg and shell products, endermite/shulker meat chains, pies, soups, salads, tea/wine/soda, three serving blocks, chorus crate and End stove. Foods also teleport, remove effects, cool the user, or apply FD/vanilla effects; these are gameplay, not mere food stats.

FD reuse is unusually deep: KnifeItem/materials, PieBlock/FeastBlock, Consumable/Popsicle/Drink/HotCocoa items, tooltip config/utilities, effects, synced tile base, recipe-manager accessor, sounds, item spawning and math. The End stove copies FD’s six-position campfire-recipe machine rather than extending its stove implementation. The port should expose the minimal reusable stove processing behavior within FD (six-slot processing/heat/render item access), allowing End-specific identity and recipes/appearance. Do not preserve a copied modern RecipeManagerAccessor dependency.

Important source caveats:

- The five registered GLM JSON definitions contain conditions/items but no `type`. They cannot be assumed to select the registered serializer correctly. Implement explicit native drop rules rather than copying malformed files.
- Knife dragon drops: legs = 2; meat = 2 plus two independent 50% extra units; teeth = 1 plus a 30% extra unit. Endermite and shulker use the same serializer class, each with 90% one-unit chance. Preserve distributions and killer/tool attribution.
- Dragon death/drop timing differs materially across targets; test actual LivingDrops/death events before deciding a deeper hook is needed. No raw ASM is warranted by this audit.
- The three unguarded `farmersrespite:brewing` recipes need a real optional integration boundary. A brewing serializer is not a vanilla potion recipe and not Brewin’s keg. Do not reroute it silently to the cooking pot. With no supported Respite port/provider, disable the brewing recipes and explicitly mark drinks that lack another acquisition path.
- Source uses MCP mappings whereas local FD is Parchment. Similar method names in pasted/decompiled code are not evidence of API compatibility. Resolve each native target’s mappings against its actual dependency artifact.
- Teleport classes distinguish crouching-only consumption (pie-slice/bubble-tea families) from unconditional wine/soup teleport. They attempt up to 16 safe teleports within a bounded range, dismount, honor the Forge teleport event where available, and apply cooldown/sounds. Preserve these conditions server-side.
- `block/ChorusFruitPie.java` is not the registered pie implementation; BlockRegistry instantiates FD PieBlock. Do not port an unused class and assume its overrides apply. The renderer reads the common horizontal-facing property through FD StoveBlock; using that shared property is not by itself proof of a bug, but it is avoidable cross-implementation coupling.

1.12.2 supplies the chorus/shulker/levitation/breath foundations. However, `registry/ModFeatures.generationChorusSucculent` filters only `minecraft:end_highlands` and `minecraft:end_midlands`, biome identities absent from 1.12.2. The [official 18w06a changelog](https://www.minecraft.net/en-us/article/minecraft-snapshot-18w06a) records the later split into distinct End biomes. Replace that selector with a native outer-End-island/substrate placement rule; do not copy the modern biome names and silently disable all succulent generation. On pre-1.9 targets without the chorus ecosystem, gate this source acquisition chain unless a real optional provider supplies it. On 1.6.4/1.7.10/1.8.9, omit those dependent chains by default. Endermite chain remains available on 1.8.9. Pearl/end-stone/dragon ingredients exist broadly, but the non-hatchable egg recipe uses the **End crystal item**, which is unavailable before 1.9; the existence of an End crystal entity does not satisfy it. Gate egg-shell derivatives too unless a real provider supplies that item. The dragon itself is not naturally respawnable through modern crystals on those versions; do not change boss progression to make meat renewable.

No later End branch was available among the local Git refs; only the audited 1.16.5 snapshot is claimed here. Do not infer later fixes/content from release names or claim that comparison complete without additional source.

### D.4 Brewin’ and Chewin’

**Difficulty:** highest. Packages span keg recipe/tile/container/inventory, item mat, cheese/pizza blocks, effects/drinks, mixins, JEI and data. Dependency graph: Brewin → FD; kombucha recipe → Farmer’s Respite tea/leaves; no dependency cycle or required link to the other three addons.

Content is keg, tankard, item mat, 15 alcoholic beverages plus kombucha, fermented foods, two cheese-wheel aging/slicing chains, pizza/slices, sandwich and fudge. Effects are Tipsy, Satisfaction and Sweet Heart. `FonduePotBlock` and FIERY_FONDUE food data exist but registration is commented out: **not shipping content**. Do not revive them merely because classes exist.

Keg authoritative path: `common/block/KegBlock` → `common/block/entity/KegBlockEntity` → `common/crafting/KegRecipe`; native screen/container and `KegItemHandler` complete it. Slots 0–3 are solid ingredients, 4 is a liquid **item** ingredient, 5 internal meal buffer, 6 serving container, 7 output. The “liquid” is not a fluid tank in this source. Matching requires exactly the solid ingredient positions plus the liquid-slot predicate; processing consumes one from each occupied slot 0–4 and ejects containers. XP/buffer/output logic parallels FD cooking.

Temperature scans the surrounding 3×3×3 cube, counts hot minus cold blocks, and selects frigid −27…−9, cold −8…−5, normal −4…4, warm 5…8, hot 9…27. In `canFerment`, category **3 explicitly bypasses** the range and therefore means unrestricted temperature in practice. Preserve this effective behavior and label it correctly, rather than imposing a new normal-only restriction. Ice/packed ice/snow/blue ice are cold in source; FD heat/tray tags are hot. Missing blue ice does not prevent the count model from working with native cold blocks; do not create substitute blue ice.

Source deficiencies and decisions:

- Keg handler accepts solid slots only from up/null, containers from other faces, and output extraction from 7. **No face inserts liquid slot 4.** Plan explicit native liquid-slot automation as a compatibility correction, with a dedicated documented side/slot policy and tests; do not claim source already automates all inputs.
- The tile rescans/allocates/sends neighbor and block updates every fermentation tick. Preserve thermal meaning, but use bounded rescans plus relevant neighbor invalidation and send only changed values. Do not claim a benchmark gain without measurements.
- All 23 fermenting resource files specify `cookingtime`; serializer reads `fermentingtime`, default 12000. Every current value is 12000, so effective timing coincides today; unify the semantic field during port and test nondefault durations.
- `kombucha` references Farmer’s Respite without declaring/gating the dependency. Gate acquisition and item exposure consistently if tea inputs are absent.
- `HideBlockBreakTextureMixin` targets client BlockRenderDispatcher but sits in the common `mixins` list. Move to client-only native rendering/hook registration. `BrewinAndChewin` also directly registers a client setup listener; isolate client class resolution deliberately in the port.
- SweetHeartEffect is registered/applied by foods but contains no behavior/attribute modifiers in this snapshot. It is an inert status marker here; do not invent healing/absorption from the name. Tipsy suppresses natural regeneration at amplifier >0 through FoodDataMixin, adds nausea above 1, stumbling motion above 3, poison above 8. Satisfaction adds one hunger on a `400 >> amplifier` tick schedule. Server must own effects and movement.
- Alcohol stacking/duration logic has multiple overlapping conditions. Establish bounded potency/amplifier behavior from source and acceptance scenarios before simplifying it; preserve durations without overflow or unintended double increments.
- Item mat’s normal use has cutting processing commented out. It displays/holds items; a copied cutting method inside its tile does not make it a second functioning cutting board. Pizza serves four slices (state 3 through 0), whereas displayed comparator values run 3 through 0; retain that distinction deliberately. Cheese ages through unripe stages into ripe wheels, then yields four knife slices.

Later local branch `upstream/1.19` is **not a safe canonical replacement**: only 27 Java files, an experimental fluid-item/tank/widget/recipe-book implementation, and most beverages, food/effect/block classes absent from the live registries. Its commits discuss water bottles, buckets, milk and fluid capability experimentation. Do not replace the complete 1.18.2 beverage/content baseline with this incomplete source simply because it is newer. Record fluid automation as a possible future feature; the requested port should first retain the item-input keg semantics.

### D.5 Content completeness findings

Every checked-out registered item/block has its corresponding item-model/blockstate file, and the bounded literal same-mod parent/texture scan found no missing targets. Every literal ecosystem item/block ID used by recipe payloads resolves to a registration somewhere in the five clones. Neither check proves the loaded dependency generation, conditional serializer, models, or survival acquisition is correct.

| Mod | Registrations without a direct recipe output | Source acquisition/use resolution and remaining flags |
|---|---|---|
| FD | Rich soil/farmland; seven wild crops; two mushroom colonies; rotten tomato; ham; four feast-serving items | Soil comes from compost/hoe conversion; wild plants from generation, shears and loot; colonies from soil/mushrooms; rotten tomatoes from harvest; ham from knife loot; serving items from feasts. Rich-farmland item exposure is an internal/creative representation, not a missing crafting recipe. Missing modern prerequisite chains still gate their dependents. |
| Corn | Wild corn | Generated plant with shears self-drop and seed acquisition. Native tall-crop upper/lower destruction requires the missing exact MMLib audit before parity can be certified. Cornbread stuffing and the Corn alternative stuffed pumpkin depend on absent sweet berries; plain popcorn does not. |
| End | Succulent, tooth, shulker/endermite meat, dragon leg, three feast servings | Generation, knife mob drops and serving supply these in intended source behavior. Missing GLM type fields and the 1.12 outer-End-biome mismatch are concrete acquisition blockers to replace. Tea recipes need a real Respite provider. This is not a clean source survival-completeness pass. |
| Brewin | Two ripe cheese wheels | Unripe wheels age into them, then knife slices provide wedges. Sweet Heart is applied but inert. Fondue is unregistered/dead content; copied mat cutting code is inactive. Kombucha has an unguarded external prerequisite; several drinks need unavailable honey/berries/Bad Omen and remain gated by target. |
| Ocean | Tentacles, guardian, elder slab, soup serving | Knife/unconditional mob modifiers and feast serving are intended roots. Missing source-generation dependency declarations and uncertain plain-Item bowl returns require correction/acceptance. Sea-pickle/kelp/seagrass/honey chains have no native path on any target. |

A tag-only use is still a legitimate use, so absence from a literal input list is not an orphan verdict. Crop internal halves, wall signs, effect registrations and rich farmland are intentionally created through native state/interaction paths. Conversely, merely finding a constant in an unused class does not establish acquisition. The full registry table retains those evidence distinctions rather than marking all content “complete.”

## E. Small shared API and ingredient/tag plan

### E.1 Reuse priority derived from actual addon references

The full import-to-consumer ledger is in Appendix 4. This table includes data-only dependencies that an import search alone would miss.

| FD subsystem/API | Actual consumers | Priority / legacy boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Cooking registration/lookup, counted ingredients, output container/time/XP | Corn, End, Ocean; keg reuses the same matching/accounting ideas | P0: canonical immutable recipe definitions and target-native stacks |
| Cutting registration/lookup, tool predicate, chance outputs | Corn, End, Ocean, Brewin cutting recipes; copied but inactive mat processing | P0: one shared recipe service, not per-addon OreDictionary scans |
| Knife membership/tools and loot predicates | End knives, Corn/Ocean cutting, all knife-specific drops, Brewin cheese | P0: explicit tool registration plus ingredient membership; no exact KnifeItem-only checks for compatible knives |
| Ingredient tags | All four, including data-only recipes | P0: stable namespaced keys, exact stack matching and narrow OreDictionary bridge |
| Foods/effects/duration and consumption/remainders | All four; End inherits several consumables, Brewin inherits DrinkableItem | P0: descriptor data plus native consumable base/helper in each FD target |
| Feast/pie serving and leftovers | End, Ocean; Brewin cheese/pizza share parts but have different rules | P1: native FD feast/pie base, small serving result primitive; avoid forcing cheese through bowl-only feasts |
| Heat-source/conductor predicates | FD machines, End stove, Brewin temperature tags | P1: reusable block predicate registry; temperature counting stays Brewin-owned |
| Stove processing and displayed slots | FD and End | P1: reusable six-position cooking behavior/native base; do not generalize into every conceivable machine |
| Synced inventory/tile base | End, Brewin | P1: native lifecycle helper; never expose a cross-version TileEntity wrapper in common |
| Crop wild-form behavior, loot configuration | Corn | P1: narrow native base/policy; Corn owns its tall-crop code |
| Add-item loot rule | Ocean, FD; End adds its own distributions | P1: typed drop-rule registration with server context; no generic modern GLM emulation engine |
| Sounds, particles, text, item spawning, small math helpers | End, Brewin; resource dependencies elsewhere | P1/P2: native utility reuse where equivalent; client utility entry points remain client-only |
| Recipe viewers | FD and Brewin have custom categories; content addons feed those categories | P0 with first machines: target-specific NEI/JEI adapters |
| Datagen builders / RecipeManagerAccessor / modern registry classes | Direct imports in newer addons | Replace dependency with legacy API. They are implementation details, not binary-compatibility obligations. |

### E.2 Proposed contract, not implemented classes

Keep the semantic surface inside FD, for example under `vectorwing.farmersdelight.api` and a small platform-neutral domain package. API names below are proposals, not claims they already exist:

- `IngredientKey`: namespaced identity such as `forge:crops/tomato`. `IngredientSpec`: exact item+metadata, NBT predicate when required, named membership, or alternatives. Preserve repeated/count requirements. A count is not encoded by duplicating OreDictionary registrations.
- `CookingRecipeDefinition` / `CuttingRecipeDefinition`: stable recipe ID, ingredient requirements, outputs/chances, serving container, time, XP and tool/sound requirements. Lookup returns a consumption plan, never consumes while testing.
- `LegacyRecipeRegistry`: explicit register/remove/replace lifecycle, duplicate-ID rejection, deterministic priority, immutable snapshots. Freeze after addons/optional integrations register; invalidate on supported script reload. An addon cannot register under another addon’s namespace accidentally.
- `KnifeRegistry` / `ToolRequirement`: matching for native tiered knives and registered third-party tools. Durability and Fortune/Looting remain native operations outside common data. Block heat and entity feed/drop membership use typed predicates, not item OreDictionary names.
- `FoodDefinition` / `ServingResult`: nutrition/effects/timing and exact container return/accounting data. Each native module implements the public consumable/feast classes addons need; the shared algorithm must not import ItemStack, World, Potion, Forge Ingredient or loader types.
- Small native services for heat checks, six-slot stove processing, loot/drop rules and viewer recipe exposure, only where the consumer table demonstrates reuse.

Do **not** add generic crop registration, a universal world API, fluid/energy networks, a scripting language, or a replacement Forge capability system to this API. Optional detection belongs in each native integration initializer; a trivial `isModLoaded` wrapper does not justify its own framework. No new mandatory runtime library JAR.

### E.3 Exhaustive tag audit and conversion rules

Appendix 3 records every checked-out JSON tag definition with kind/namespace/value and every resource tag use, plus source helper declarations. Item tags, block tags, entity tags and integration metadata are distinct registries. `forge:tools/knives` and `farmersdelight:tools/knives` overlap but are not automatically identical; deliberately map both to the canonical knife service. Preserve `replace`/optional-entry semantics at registration time. A tag with no entries is an unsatisfied ingredient, not a wildcard.

| Ingredient concept | Modern source keys | Legacy registration / accepted alias policy |
|---|---|---|
| Tomato / onion / cabbage / rice / corn | `forge:crops/*`, grain/rice/corn | Register FD/Corn-owned produce as `cropTomato`, `cropOnion`, `cropCabbage`, `cropRice`, `cropCorn`; accept those exact aliases. Do not equate a rice panicle with a rice grain. |
| Seeds | `forge:seeds/*` | Register/accept explicit seed keys such as `seedTomato`, `seedCabbage`, `seedCorn` as project conventions; rice remains its plantable grain, not a fabricated seed item. |
| Dough / raw pasta / bread / tortilla / popcorn | `forge:dough` (newer FD), `forge:pasta/raw_pasta`, `forge:bread*`, Corn keys | FD owns dough membership with its wheat dough; accept evidenced `foodDough`, `foodPasta`, `foodTortilla` only where semantically equal. Use `foodBread`, `foodPopcorn` as proposed opt-in export names, not universal Forge standards. |
| Milk / eggs | `forge:milk`, `milk/milk`, `milk/milk_bottle`, `forge:eggs` | Exact native milk bucket + FD bottle; accept `listAllmilk`/`listAllegg` through adapters. Keep bucket/bottle portion identity and returned containers; do not convert arbitrary fluid amounts into one recipe unit. |
| Raw/cooked species meats | `forge:raw_*`, `cooked_*`, fish subgroups | Exact vanilla/FD stacks plus validated `listAllbeefraw`, `listAllbeefcooked`, etc. A beef recipe stays beef, not `listAllmeat`. Bacon is not interchangeable with a whole porkchop unless that source tag permits it. |
| Vegetables / salad / fruit | `forge:vegetables*`, `salad_ingredients*`, `cabbage_roll_ingredients` | Curated union matching source membership. Broad `listAllveggie`/`listAllfruit` aliases are candidate integrations, not permission to accept any edible crop. Retain cabbage-specific membership for wraps. |
| Knives, axes, pickaxes, shovels, shears | `forge:tools/*`, FD knives/straw harvesters | Tool predicate service + explicit registrations; do not match wooden crafting ingredients or every sword. `toolKnife` can be an opt-in export alias, never the only authoritative test. |
| Bowls / bottles / tankards / shell containers | Output container stacks and serving tags | Exact native/FD/addon container or explicitly registered equivalent with matching portion size. No broad `container` alias. |
| Heat / conductor / compost / soil / feed users | FD block/entity tags; Brewin hot/cold | Typed block state + entity predicates, not OreDictionary ItemStack entries. Lit-state predicates and subgroup inclusion remain intact. |
| Create / IE / Botany Pots / Serene Seasons / Diet | External recipes/tags | Target-specific optional integrations only; absence must not load their classes, register their serializer IDs, or affect core recipes. |

Evidence: Forge documents OreDictionary ingredients and startup-only recipe conditions for 1.12 at [Forge recipe documentation](https://docs.minecraftforge.net/en/1.12.x/utilities/recipes/). A preserved [HarvestCraft implementation](https://github.com/MrCrayfish/harvestcraft/blob/master/java/com/pam/harvestcraft/PamFoodRecipes.java) demonstrates crop and `listAll*`/food naming traditions; it is evidence of conventions, not a required dependency or proof that every pack uses every name.

**Per-target policy:** OreDictionary exists on all four targets. The proposed export/alias names above can be kept consistent across targets, but the audit did not verify a particular installed provider/version on any of them. In 1.6.4 and 1.8.9 especially, do not claim that a popular 1.7.10/1.12.2 food alias is prepopulated. Register FD’s own entries, bridge third-party names only when present, and validate exact metadata. On 1.7.10/1.8.9/1.12.2 fish subtypes distinguish cod, salmon, clownfish and pufferfish; on 1.6.4 only generic native fish is available. Do not wildcard fish metadata to unlock absent fugu.

**Duplicate/conflict handling:** register each owned item/damage/NBT membership once; avoid adding wildcard metadata for a damageable tool or multi-variant fish. Import aliases into local membership sets rather than rewriting another mod’s OreDictionary categories. Sort/deduplicate resolved candidates by registry identity; recipes retain their own counts. Validate alias cycles, missing required entries and optional namespaces at registry freeze. Late OreRegisterEvent additions invalidate membership snapshots where supported; clients receive the server’s effective recipe snapshot rather than independently choosing pack semantics.

**Fallback:** native FD produce, milk, egg, dough and knives make the baseline independent of other food mods. If a required ingredient genuinely does not exist (honey, chorus, kelp), disable that recipe and its dependent acquisition chain with a recorded reason. Never substitute sugar for honey, squid for guardian, ordinary mushroom for crimson fungus, or generic fish for pufferfish.

Combined cells identify different layers. None means already implemented.
