| End dragon-breath collection and foods | X/O or I | X/O or I | X/O or I | D/A |
| End non-hatchable egg/shell chain (End crystal ingredient) | X/O or I | X/O or I | X/O or I | D/A |
| End stove, even on reduced-content targets | R | R | R | R |
| End teleport consumables (when ingredients exist) | R: safe native teleport | R | R | A |
| End / Brewin Farmer’s Respite recipes | I: no provider cloned | I | I | I |
| Brewin keg item inputs/temp/XP/containers | R | R | R | R/A |
| Brewin ordinary cheese/pizza/jerky recipes with native inputs | R/A | R/A | R/A | R/A |
| Brewin Tipsy/Satisfaction | R | R | R | R |
| Brewin Sweet Heart | D: inert source marker | D | D | D |
| Brewin Dread Nog Bad Omen effect | X/O or I | X/O or I | X/O or I | X/O or I |
| Brewin blue-ice membership | X/O; other cold blocks remain | X/O | X/O | X/O |
| Brewin later experimental fluid keg | Separate redesign; not baseline | Separate redesign | Separate redesign | Separate redesign |
| NEI/JEI custom machines | NEI native handler | NEI native handler | JEI native plugin | JEI native plugin |
| Modern advancement criteria | R: achievements/stat events | R | R | A: native advancements |

Feature gating is transitive: an absent ingredient disables recipes, associated drops and viewer entries; a dependent item without another acquisition route is marked unavailable/hidden for new play. Keep already-saved stacks/IDs conservatively when configuration/providers change. Never replace unknown NBT or removed mappings with air. A target’s support ledger must list the exact unavailable item and recipe IDs, not merely say “reduced content.” Appendix 2 supplies the starting ID-by-ID recipe ledger.

### F.1 Later FD content is a deliberate extension ledger

FD upstream 1.18.2 (`fbb803a02c82363dc6106ac36aef045a0998ac71`, 1.2.3) adds bone broth, mushroom rice, cod/salmon/kelp rolls and slices, rice-roll medley, glow-berry custard, sandy shrub and rope-supported tomato stages; pantries become cabinets. Include applicable bug fixes (recipe-swap progress, remainder overrides, soup-stack handling, seed/item identity), retain old registry names with explicit mapping if adopting renames, and schedule newer dishes/plant behavior as a visible extension tranche. Kelp/glow berries remain unavailable without providers on every target.

FD upstream 1.20 (`e854d56d4eea8dc120650d43da0d1472711797ad`, 1.3.4) and 1.21 (`dd8b9b0bfec916a2da88a6d64e3a37f4a02c3259`, 1.3.4) add wooden/bamboo baskets, rope fences/gates, onion soup, pumpkin-pie slices, gleaming salad/feast, hanging canvas signs and more wood cabinets. These include new ingredients/wood species and are not all trivial legacy copies. Their histories/source also correct feast piston behavior, sign NBT copying, rope reeling boundaries, tomato packet handling, and NBT/component-sensitive cooking output. Apply the correctness invariant where the old feature exists; do not import modern component registries, EMI or networking merely to copy a fix.

Proposed release baseline: checked-out content, gated by real target prerequisites; applicable correctness fixes; Corn drink/stuffing corrections. Later additions above remain explicitly deferred, not silently omitted. An expanded-content release can select them after the baseline is accepted. Recipe/balance changes such as later chicken-soup/fried-rice nerfs are gameplay decisions, not automatic bug fixes.

## G. Recipe architecture and loot implementation

### G.1 Recipe families and target-native registration

| Family / observed use | 1.12.2 | 1.8.9 | 1.7.10 | 1.6.4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Shaped/shapeless crafting; compression, feasts, food assembly | Native IRecipe/registry; optional translated `assets/<modid>/recipes` JSON | Java IRecipe/Ore recipes | Java GameRegistry/Ore recipes | Native Java recipes and explicit ID/config lookup |
| Furnace smelting | Java native smelting registration | Same native family | Same native family | Same native family |
| Smoker / blast / campfire | No vanilla stations; retain separate FD semantic cook families for FD stations, gate absent station-only recipes | Same | Same | Same |
| FD cooking pot | FD recipe registry + native Ingredient/stack adapter | FD registry + native adapter | FD registry + OreDictionary adapter | FD registry + metadata/ID adapter |
| FD cutting | Same chance/tool model; native handler | Same | Same | Same |
| Skillet / FD stove / End stove | Consume FD campfire-style cooking definitions, preserving time/output and station factors | Same with legacy rendering/inventory | Same | Same |
| Brewin fermenting | Brewin-owned recipe type using FD ingredients; 4 solids + liquid item/temp/time | Same native tile/container | Same | Same, Java-7-compatible semantics |
| End Respite brewing | Optional real provider’s API; otherwise gate | Gate absent provider | Gate absent provider | Gate absent provider |
| Feast/cheese/pizza serving | World interaction transaction; recipe viewers can expose serving information | Same | Same | Same, metadata/block identity state |
| Netherite smithing | Gate missing prerequisite, optional true provider only | Same | Same | Same |
| Special/manual actions | Native crop/knife/milking/projectile interactions; no fake crafting substitute | Same | Same | Same |
| Conditional / external mod machines | Resolve startup mod/config/content predicates before registration | Same | Same | Same |

Do not collapse multiple recipe IDs for different cooking stations into duplicate furnace entries. A shared output does not imply the same cooking time or XP. Source has smoker 100, furnace 200 and campfire 600 tick examples. Keep station-specific semantic records even when only FD’s own stove/skillet can consume the campfire family. Do not add ordinary furnace recipes for an intentionally station-only process merely to make it obtainable.

The appendix preserves exact ingredients, patterns, counts, secondary chances, containers, time, XP, temperature and conditions. Java registration on the older targets can encode those records directly. Do not introduce a generic modern datapack parser solely to preserve the syntax of checked-in JSON. 1.12 crafting JSON lives under **assets**, not the later `data/<modid>/recipes` path; smelting remains Java registration. See [Forge’s native 1.12 recipe rules](https://docs.minecraftforge.net/en/1.12.x/utilities/recipes/).

Recipe matching must consume atomically after checking output capacity and containers. Use exact matching/backtracking for the small ≤6 requirement set; do not let broad tags greedily consume the only candidate for a narrower ingredient. Recipe result stacks are copied, never mutable global registry instances. IDs are stable across targets where the recipe exists; different station IDs remain distinct. Freeze registration after optional integrations, with duplicate IDs rejected except an explicit replace operation.

### G.2 Loot/drop categories and conversion

| Category | Source ownership | Native legacy implementation |
|---|---|---|
| Ordinary mob additions | FD GLMs; Ocean guardian | Target LivingDrops event with server-side native entity predicate and one registered rule execution |
| Knife-specific mob additions | FD scavenging; Ocean tentacles/elder slab; End meats | Match responsible killer/tool/looting context, not nearest player. Reconcile death-time weapon vs held stack behavior with the source predicate; test indirect/projectile/automation kills. |
| Knife grass/crop straw | FD modifier conditions | Native block drops/harvest events preserving maturity, actual tool, explosion/silk/fortune conditions and cancellation |
| Cake/pie slicing | FD interaction + pastry loot modifier | Distinguish right-click one-slice serving from breaking entire pastry with a knife. Prevent both paths firing for one action. |
| Chest/village/structure injection | FD tables; Corn injected tables | 1.12 LootTableLoadEvent/native tables; older ChestGenHooks or native structure loot integration where the target offers the same chest. Do not pretend modern village-house families exist. |
| Crop/wild/feast/block drops | Native block loot JSON | Target-native getDrops/harvest/quantity/metadata logic, with one owner for tall-crop destruction and leftovers |
| Machine-carried data | FD copy meal/skillet; Brewin copy meal | Explicit break/drop NBT transfer, separate loose inventories, exact XP lifecycle |
| Fishing | No custom fishing rule found | No added integration; native fishing acquisition remains relevant for salmon/pufferfish |

Preserve source chance/count conditions and looting/fire/smoker processing where present; do not apply a uniform “one extra drop” policy. Dragon death is a specific runtime checkpoint. Avoid injecting at a convenient common entity method without verifying the dragon actually traverses that path.

## H. Rendering and state representation plan

Most assets are static JSON models/blockstates and PNG textures. All checked-out registered item/block model paths are present. Static inspection also found no unresolved same-mod literal parent/texture paths; this does not check UV correctness, texture-variable resolution, external parents, animation, actual atlas loading or any rendered scene.

| System | Source rendering/state | Legacy strategy |
|---|---|---|
| Crops/wild plants/colonies/compost | Age/cluster/state JSON, cutout | 1.8.9/1.12 native baked model/block states; 1.6.4/1.7.10 metadata-indexed icons and ordinary crossed/custom block geometry. No tiles solely for crop age. |
| Feast/pie/cheese/pizza | Facing + serving/bite geometry | Static models/native metadata per state. Five servings × four facings needs 20 states: split full/empty states or directional block identities where needed; do not truncate to 4-bit metadata. |
| Pantry/basket | Facing/open/enabled; static body | Keep real inventory tile, render body ordinarily; derive nonpersisted connection/open state when safe. Avoid a second state store disagreeing with tile inventory. |
| Canvas sign | Sign text + material TESR | Real native sign tile/edit sync, static colored backgrounds and text TESR; sign orientation/text require their actual data, not a decorative surrogate. |
| FD board | Static board plus item/tool TESR | Ordinary block body + TESR for stored item, flat/embedded transform and facing. |
| FD/End stove | Static lit/facing body plus six displayed items | Native body render + item-only TESR; preserve six offsets, orientation and heat/obstruction state. |
| Skillet | Block/item model overrides, carried enchantment, item stack visuals, particles/sizzle | Native custom item renderer where needed; placed item TESR. No global dynamic renderer for otherwise static items. |
| Brewin mat | Item display renderer with bob/rotation | Native item TESR reading sync state; animation is client-only and does not mutate stored item. |
| Rope/net/rug/tatami | Connected/multipart/state models, transparency | Native connections and cutout geometry; neighbor-derived connection state avoids wasting persisted metadata. Preserve fall/collision behavior separately. |
| Rotten tomato | Item projectile + particles | Native entity/item renderer registered only client-side; server tracks motion and impact. |
| Overlays/screens/JEI/NEI | Effect HUD and GUI widgets | Target-native GUI scale, lighting and render-state restoration; test alongside overlays and recipe viewers. |

No GeckoLib or similar required animation library was found. Do not add one. Texture path conventions differ (legacy `textures/blocks` / `textures/items` versus modern singular directories); preserve stable source asset keys in common resources only where translation is mechanical. Target-owned blockstates, metadata maps, GUI classes, TESRs and item-render interfaces remain native.

For 1.8.9/1.12.2 the listed/persisted block-state space still has a 4-bit metadata projection; modern BlockState syntax does not magically provide unlimited persistent combinations. Plan all serving/facing/water/connection combinations before assigning IDs. Canvas signs naturally keep text in a tile; do not use a tile for an empty feast merely to evade a small state-budget calculation.

## I. Networking, server safety, viewers, and invasive hooks

### I.1 Source findings and target contracts

No custom general-purpose channel was found in the checked-out five sources. FD uses native recipe serialization, tile update packets/tags, menu data slots, GUI opening and a Forge projectile spawn packet. End relies on tile updates and server teleport logic. Brewin uses the same native mechanisms for its keg/mat, with a misplaced client mixin as noted. Corn and Ocean mostly use block updates and inherited FD behavior. This is not evidence that no networking work is needed: pre-datapack targets do not automatically synchronize a new custom recipe registry.

Define a small FD-owned recipe snapshot protocol for server-defined custom recipes, sent after login and after an explicitly supported reload. It carries stable IDs, resolved ingredient alternatives, quantities/chances/containers/time/XP and addon recipe categories. Version/channel identifiers and bounded lengths protect decoding; absent optional items are filtered server-side. Native recipe viewers consume this snapshot. No client computes authoritative availability from its local config alone. Disconnect clears world/server-specific recipe data; changing servers cannot retain the previous recipe set.

Menus use native container click transactions and progress updates. Client requests contain intent/position/window identity, never an accepted result stack. The server verifies the player’s open menu, distance, current block/tile and inventory before mutation. Unsolicited client-supplied tile NBT cannot overwrite inventory. Schedule packet work on the owning server/client thread where the native networking API invokes handlers off-thread. For 1.6.4 use its actual custom-payload/FML packet route; do not copy SimpleNetworkWrapper from 1.7.10 unchanged.

Tiles save authoritative inventory/progress/recipe IDs/containers; clients receive only required render/menu state. Preserve dirty marking, chunk reload, capability invalidation on 1.12, and tile removal/replacement. All render/model/HUD/recipe-viewer classes are client-only entry points. A client import in an item used only by a stripped tooltip method is not automatically a server crash, but shared entrypoint resolution and common mixin lists need explicit isolation. No runtime dedicated-server safety is certified by this audit.

### I.2 Viewer plan at machine implementation time

| Target | Viewer boundary | Required categories |
|---|---|---|
| 1.6.4 | Optional native NEI plugin/handler; pin that generation’s actual CodeChicken APIs | Pot, cutting, decomposition info; later keg with liquid-item slot and effective temperature |
| 1.7.10 | Optional native NEI handler; verify against selected stock/maintained fork API rather than assuming method descriptors | Same, with chance secondary outputs/tooltips/catalysts |
| 1.8.9 | Optional JEI plugin for that generation | Same; native container transfer must preserve counted ingredients |
| 1.12.2 | Optional JEI plugin for that generation | Same; show containers and missing/gated recipe handling |

FD source JEI includes cooking/cutting/decomposition, pot transfer, GUI click area, stove/skillet campfire catalysts, and acquisition info for straw/ham/knives. Brewin source has fermentation category/catalyst/click area, but its transfer handler is commented out. Content-only addons should register through FD recipes and appear automatically. Do not hardcode separate Corn/Ocean viewers with divergent ingredient logic. An absent viewer must not prevent server boot. Early viewer integration is an implementation acceptance aid, not a substitute for machine execution tests. Exact viewer artifact versions must be pinned against the selected target before coding those adapters; current modern JEI dependency strings are unusable there.

### I.3 Mixin qualification

Most modern hooks disappear by owning the native implementation or using Forge events. Remaining candidates are global vanilla soup stack/return behavior, breeding ingredients where events do not suffice, rich-soil protection from native tree replacement, and Brewin’s natural-regeneration interception. Check the actual vanilla/native call path first.

- **1.7.10:** prefer UniMixins with the existing GTNH build ecosystem. Its official [README](https://github.com/LegacyModdingMC/UniMixins/blob/master/README.md) describes its compatibility role; [developer guidance](https://github.com/LegacyModdingMC/UniMixins/blob/master/docs/developer-usage.md) recommends the GTNH example setup. Enable/pin the integration deliberately; the workspace template currently says `usesMixins=false`.
- **1.8.9 / 1.12.2:** MixinBooter is a candidate; its [official README](https://github.com/CleanroomMC/MixinBooter/blob/main/README.md) describes 1.8–1.12.2 support. Qualify a release for stock Forge/Java 8, required targets and transformation order before pinning it. Do not substitute Cleanroom’s newer-Java runtime for the requested Forge runtime.
- **1.6.4:** no compatible Mixin loader/bootstrap was established by this audit. UniMixins’ documented scope does not include it. First qualify available source/runtime against stock LaunchWrapper/ASM 4.1 and Java-7-scannable mod classes. FD-owned food/recipes/machines can proceed without global vanilla transforms. If no maintainable bootstrap can implement global soup behavior, record that particular config feature as unavailable rather than shipping a guessed raw transformer. This is a bounded architectural checkpoint, not permission to abandon the rest of the target.

No raw ASM is recommended. A hook implementation must verify descriptors, mappings, target inheritance, bootstrap timing, optional absence, refmaps and remapped packaged JAR behavior. Client-only mixins stay in the client list. Do not carry a modern Mixin `JAVA_16` setting or Java 17 class into the oldest targets.

## J. Repository, build, release and persistence architecture

### J.1 Separate repositories versus ecosystem monorepo

| Concern | Separate multiversion mod repos (recommended) | Giant ecosystem repo |
|---|---|---|
| Builds | Isolated target/tooling per mod; FD dev API artifact consumed by addons | More convenient cross-mod changes but many targets and loader plugins to isolate |
| Releases/versioning | Independent addon updates and FD API compatibility range | Requires strict artifact separation to avoid coupled version/release cadence |
| Dependencies | Directed FD → addon build ordering; pin dev and runtime variants | Composite/subproject dependency setup still needs per-target mappings |
| Shared code | Small FD API; no duplicated common library at runtime | Easy to accidentally merge addon code into base mod |
| Testing/CI | Matrix per mod+target; selected combined smoke packs | One central combined matrix but expensive fan-out and coupling |
| Publishing | Independent CurseForge/Modrinth projects and dependency metadata | Multiple release jobs/artifacts still required |
| Upstream/licensing | Per-addon lineage/license preserved; Ocean can be withheld independently | License ambiguity and experimental branches can contaminate shared release scope |
| Maintenance | Reuse template conventions, explicitly propagate infrastructure fixes | Central infrastructure easier; broader source ownership/review boundaries |

No strong source evidence justifies overriding the requested preference for separate repositories. CI should build FD first, publish a versioned **developer** dependency for each MC target, then compile addons against the matching native API. Do not put an obfuscated runtime FD JAR on a development compile classpath or ship development JARs to players. A pack smoke job composes released/remapped artifacts, not classes from unrelated development launches.

### J.2 Match the inspected workspace conventions

`Examplemod-Multiversion/README.md`, the four target build/settings/property files, shared conventions, Python coordinator and `verification.json` were inspected. These are local pinned conventions, not a proposal to upgrade the current upstream builds in place today.

| Target | Forge baseline | Build system / wrapper | Build JVM / compiler / mod runtime |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.6.4 | 9.11.1.1345 | Unimined 1.4.1 / Gradle 8.13 | Build JDK 21; compiler JDK 17 `--release 7`; class major ≤51; game Java 8 |
| 1.7.10 | 10.13.4.1614 | GTNHGradle 2.0.34 / RFG 2.0.6 / Gradle 9.7.1 | Build JDK 25; JDK 17/Jabel; Java 8 runtime APIs/major 52 |
| 1.8.9 | 11.15.1.2318 | Unimined 1.4.1 / Gradle 8.13 | Build/compiler JDK 21, Java 8 API/bytecode, Java 8 game |
| 1.12.2 | 14.23.5.2859 | Unimined 1.4.1 / Gradle 8.13 | Build/compiler JDK 21, Java 8 API/bytecode, Java 8 game |

The root coordinates independent target wrappers rather than loading all Forge plugins into one daemon. Keep the release/version transaction, archive naming, remap/reobf tasks, required-class/resource checks and isolated dependency resolution. Copy only the four requested target configurations, not all eight template targets or example gameplay. Do not upgrade any pin just because an audit dependency is easier with a newer tool.

Important template adaptation: its common module is Java 8, but 1.6.4 production source compiles with Java 7. Any domain source compiled into all four FD targets must therefore be **Java 7 language/API compatible**, or be genuinely split into an oldest-target-native implementation. Do not ship Java 8 common classes inside a Java 7-scannable 1.6.4 archive. Configure the common verification baseline deliberately for this ecosystem. No lambdas, default methods, streams, records or Java 8 collection APIs in that shared subset.

Proposed layout per repository:

```text
common/src/main/java/       Small Java-7-compatible semantic algorithms/data, only if actually reused
common/assets/             Compatible PNG/localization source data, not target blockstate files
versions/1.6.4/             Native Forge classes/resources/settings/wrapper
versions/1.7.10/
versions/1.8.9/
versions/1.12.2/
build-logic/               Target packaging/resource/version conventions
scripts/                   Existing-style target build coordinator/JDK selection
verification.json          Mod identity, required native/domain classes, metadata/assets
version.properties         Per-mod release metadata
```

**Shared candidates with concrete value:** recipe IDs and semantic descriptors; ingredient-match/consumption planning; chance-output math; effect/food numeric descriptions; compatibility feature decisions; serving/container accounting where native behavior is equivalent. These have no Minecraft imports. Addon common layers may be empty for small native content; avoid a common module solely to host a constant.

**Native candidates:** entrypoint/registries; ItemStack/NBT conversion; item/block/entity/potion classes; world mutation and heat/block/entity predicates; crop/soil/village generation; capabilities/inventories; GUI/menu; packets; sounds/particles/TESRs; NEI/JEI/optional integrations; metadata models; achievements/advancements. FD’s public native consumable/feast/knife interfaces can use that target’s Minecraft types; they must not leak into the shared module.

Common recipe descriptors are authored data/Java records in the ordinary sense, not generated merged source trees or preprocessor directives. Do not introduce Stonecutter, symlinks, or a universal cross-version facade. Identical semantics can share small algorithms while native machine/storage classes differ substantially.

### J.3 IDs and persistence

Preserve `farmersdelight`, `corn_delight`, `ends_delight`, `brewinandchewin`, `oceansdelight` and content paths where practical. 1.6.4 requires configurable numeric block/item/potion/enchantment allocation with reserved ranges and collision checks; 1.7.10 still has practical registry/potion/metadata limits. Do not require an ID-extender mod without evidence that the content needs it. FD’s 86 blocks plus addons and planned state splits must be budgeted before committing layouts.

Use a versioned tile-NBT schema for legacy implementations, preserving named inventories/progress/XP/containers and reading any explicitly supported predecessor format. There is no implicit world downgrade from 1.16.5 to 1.6.4; matching item names is not a guarantee of cross-version world import. Never silently clamp old stock, discard item NBT, reset unknown recipe IDs to another recipe, or reroll recorded outputs during load. If a saved recipe becomes unavailable, retain the inventory/progress safely and stop processing until resolved.

Adopting pantry→cabinet or basket→new basket IDs requires an explicit remapping decision and save migration; otherwise retain checked-out names. Native content that depends on an absent optional provider must not erase existing saves when the provider is removed. New-world content exposure and saved-ID preservation are different concerns.

## K. Exact phased roadmap and acceptance strategy

These are implementation phases, not work performed by this audit. Build once the coherent phase is complete; use cheap static checks during editing. A final source change invalidates earlier compile/package evidence. Each target is independently certified. Use the existing template test support for small pure-domain tests only; do not create fake mods, ad hoc Java harnesses or a second validation framework.

| Milestone | Paired implementation scope | Gate before proceeding |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1A — Foundation | Native item/block registration, food definitions/effects, knives, configuration, ingredients/OreDictionary, duplicate-rejecting recipe registry, minimal JEI/NEI | Both compile/package and dedicated servers start; equivalent IDs, aliases, knife/food/container behavior; duplicate rejection; viewers enumerate custom registrations; no 1.12 API in shared code |
| 1B — Cutting | Board, recipes, tool predicates, per-unit chance outputs, Fortune, durability, dispenser fallback, JEI/NEI | Equivalent player/dispenser processing, chances, tool damage and presentation on both |
| 1C — Cooking | Pot, six inputs, internal meal buffer, containers/output, heat, sided automation, comparator, XP, persistence, synchronization and viewers | Both versions exhibit equivalent gameplay; do not call the architecture stable after only one passes |
| 1D — Farming and serving | Cabbage/onion/tomato/rice, wild crops, rich soil, compost, feasts, pies, container returns, Comfort/Nourishment in their complete acquisition paths | Equivalent gameplay semantics with deliberate native mechanics; food/effect acceptance including Modern Regen on 1.7.10 |
| 1E — Secondary systems | Stove/skillet, pantries, basket, crates/bales, rope, canvas signs, rotten tomato, loot, applicable villages/trades | Both complete the feasible baseline and its interaction/persistence matrix |
| Later FD targets | 1.8.9, then 1.6.4 | Independent native builds, artifacts and gameplay acceptance; explicit unavailable content gates |
| Addons | Corn → Ocean (license/content permitted) → End → Brewin | First establish paired FD core. Corn and Brewin pair 1.12.2/1.7.10; Ocean pairs feasible subsets; End starts on 1.12.2 with an explicitly reduced/gated 1.7.10 counterpart. A single working target is not “legacy supported.” |

For every major system: define expected behavior → implement 1.12.2 → implement 1.7.10 immediately → run equivalent acceptance checks → resolve architectural issues → only then mark the shared subsystem stable. No Cooking Pot or Cutting Board implementation begins until 1A passes. A successful build alone does not pass that gate.

### K.1 Automated/headless checks to add during implementation

Use ordinary tests around actual semantic classes, not tests that mirror assignments. Each test must prove a user-visible invariant. The explicit request for testing supports targeted test additions in the future implementation, but no test infrastructure was added for this audit.

| Invariant | Minimum meaningful cases |
|---|---|
| IDs / registration completeness | No duplicate IDs; conditional recipes evaluated before missing registry lookups; all enabled registry references resolve; expected target gates listed |
| Ingredient matching | Repeated ingredients; broad+narrow overlapping tags; exact fish metadata; missing alias; alias cycle; no wildcard tool-damage matches; all-or-nothing consumption |
| Recipe outputs | Source counts, secondary chances and per-unit Fortune rolls; buffer full/incompatible NBT; no mutating a shared recipe output stack |
| Containers | Ingredient bucket/bowl remainder distinct from serving bowl; stacked foods; empty/full inventory; creative; custom End shell and Brewin tankard; no duplicate return |
| Progress / XP | Recipe swap resets; lost heat decay; reload; player extraction clears once; hopper leaves XP; breaking pays once; removed recipe stays safe |
| Food/effects | Exact hunger/saturation/timing, actual medium=3600, fast/always-eat, Corn 12 hunger correction, Tipsy thresholds, Satisfaction schedule |
| Feature gating | Every target’s absent vanilla prerequisites; provider absent/present/removed; Respite recipe gated; gated items hidden without deleting saved stacks |
| Sync | Deterministic server recipe snapshot; protocol bounds; wrong menu/distance rejected; disconnect/rejoin clears stale viewer data |

Probability tests should use controlled RNG values or exact distribution arithmetic, not flaky wall-clock sampling. Network/thread safety and native entity/world behavior require the runtime matrix too; a pure test cannot certify them.

### K.2 Manual in-game acceptance checklists

Run each enabled target first on a **dedicated server**, then with two clients. Accept the Minecraft EULA on the user’s behalf for server tests. Use packaged remapped/reobfuscated mod artifacts and the named runtime JDK; development launches alone do not certify distribution.

**Every mod/target:** boot with minimal dependencies; login/rejoin; open each GUI; break/replace blocks; save/reload chunk and restart server; verify no client class loading or missing optional classes; inspect all enabled registry items/models/recipes in the native viewer; repeat with viewer absent; test another player observing interactions. Configuration changes must agree with the server’s effective recipe/content rules.

**FD:** cook with six distinct/duplicate ingredients; wrong/full output and bowls; top/side/bottom insertion/extraction; manual serving, shift-click, XP and pot-carried meals; change recipe/heat mid-progress. Cut with knife/axe/shears and wrong tool; hopper board input/output; dispenser and non-board fallback; Fortune secondary outputs. Plant/bonemeal/harvest each crop; rice lower/upper break, water restoration and obstructed growth; tomato repeat harvest; wild-plant shears/loot. Compost environments/soil growth/trampling/colonies. Eat all food families, fast snacks and every drink; returned container with full inventory. Serve every feast/pie to zero and recover leftovers; comparator states. Six-slot stove, placed/handheld skillet controls; pantry/basket transfer/redstone, crates, signs/colors/text, ropes/nets, feed and projectile. Test all enabled mob/chest drops and native village integration.

**Corn:** acquire wild corn in configured biomes, shear vs break it; plant and grow past age 4; break top vs bottom at each age, bonemeal and Fortune without double drops; rich-soil compatibility; harvest/replant. Cook/cut/compress/decompress; all bowls/bottles and effects; creamy drink animation; 12-hunger stuffing. Optional machine integrations absent/present only when supported. Test crop/chest config policies and any later chosen nachos/popcorn state separately.

**Ocean:** squid knife vs ordinary/indirect kill; guardian vs elder native predicate; fugu exact metadata and multi-output cutting; rolls and wraps; pot soup-block output then all feast portions and bowls. Confirm aquatic chains are hidden/unregistered consistently where no provider exists. Addon without another addon must still work.

**End:** acquire dragon ingredients using an actual completed boss death, wrong tool and second player; verify counts and probability rules rather than granting items. Endermite/shulker knives, succulent biome/bonemeal, all knives/durability. Six-slot End stove obstruction/heat/orientation/save. Test all teleports crouching and standing, low/high height, blocked destinations, riding, cancellation, cooldown, rejoin and observer sync. Egg-shell/bowl serving and leftovers; Respite absent/present. Test unavailability of chorus/egg chains on old targets without invented prerequisites.

**Brewin:** ferment all categories at each boundary (−9/−8/−5/−4/4/5/8/9), with category 3 unrestricted; add/remove sources and relog; empty/wrong liquid item; automate all documented faces including corrected liquid input; full output/buffer, custom containers, ingredient remainder, recipe swap, XP and keg-carry/break. Drink repeated potency combinations; verify server-owned Tipsy stumbling/natural regen suppression/nausea/poison and Satisfaction; Sweet Heart remains an inert marker unless separately redesigned. Cheese aging and four slices, pizza four slices/comparator, mat display/removal; tea/honey/berries/Bad Omen gates; viewer absence and category visibility.

## L. Risk register, licensing and audit limits

### L.1 Prioritized risks

| Risk | Severity | Evidence / mitigation | Exit criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| Missing vanilla content causes fake parity/unobtainable chains | High | F matrix + per-recipe ledger; real-provider integration or explicit transitive gate | Every enabled recipe’s prerequisites acquired natively in acceptance |
| FD API generations differ | High | Older root packages vs newer `common.*`; addon imports include implementation internals | All addons call the small legacy API on every target, no copied modern RecipeManager internals |
| Corn inherited MMLib logic missing | High | Four external types own food/crop behavior | Exact dependency inspected before replacement, food/tall-crop acceptance specified |
| Ocean redistribution rights unclear | High / release blocker | MIT metadata versus Forge LGPL boilerplate; no resolved author grant | Authoritative applicable license/permission for code/assets or omit shipping |
| 1.6.4 stock ASM scans bytecode before Java 8 runtime can load it | High | Template deliberately uses Java 7 major 51 | Final production archive verified against stock ASM; common baseline corrected |
| Numeric ID/potion/enchantment limits | High on oldest | FD+addons+state split budget; no required extenders established | Stable configurable allocation/collision errors and large-pack test |
| Metadata/state overflow | High | Feast 5×4=20, crop upper bits, sign orientations | Complete persistence/render state map round-trips without aliasing |
| OreDictionary broad/conflicting aliases | Medium/high | Food portions/species/damage metadata matter | Deterministic typed membership; no wildcard/default match on absence |
| Dedicated server classloading | High | Brewin renderer mixin common; direct client listener references | Packaged dedicated boot/login/menus with viewers absent succeeds |
| Container duplication/loss and NBT merging | High | Pot/keg buffers, ordinary Item foods, later upstream fixes | Player/automation/creative/full-inventory/restart matrix passes |
| Boss death bypasses ordinary drop event | High for End | Target-dependent dragon death sequence | Actual boss death awards each configured rule once |
| Recipe sync/viewer mismatch | High | Modern RecipeManager sync cannot be copied to old custom types | Client viewer and server execute same recipes/ingredients after rejoin |
| Optional integrations become hard dependencies | Medium/high | Respite unguarded recipes; optional modern resource formats | No unsupported serializer/class loads without provider |
| Addon dependency cycle | Low in current snapshot | Only FD-required graph plus optional external recipes | Preserve acyclic startup/build order; no FD dependency back onto addons |
| Rendering divergence | Medium | JSON→legacy geometry, TESRs, metadata | Visual acceptance for every serving/growth/facing state |
| Unqualified deep hooks | High on 1.6.4 | No verified Mixin bootstrap there | Prove loader/mappings/remap on stock runtime or declare specific unavailable behavior |
| Source-generation regression | Medium/high | BC 1.19 incomplete; FD/Corn later fixes/content | Chosen baseline + explicit corrections/extensions reviewed by IDs |
| Aggregate success hides skipped targets | High | Four independent toolchains/artifacts | Verify each expected target task and final artifact, not just root exit code |

### L.2 License/attribution decisions

FD, Corn, End and Brewin each contain an actual MIT grant in their root license, with the copyright owners recorded in B. Preserve that notice and permission text with redistributed copies/substantial portions, and retain contributor credits. No conflicting copyright/SPDX grant was found in the checked-out Java files; do not infer that an absent header waives the repository license. FD’s ChanceResult explicitly credits Create’s implementation; retain relevant attribution and verify any separately copied upstream code’s own license rather than importing it casually.

Ocean’s checked-in `LICENSE.txt` begins by licensing Minecraft Forge/FML under LGPL 2.1 and includes third-party sections, while its addon metadata states MIT. That boilerplate does not clearly identify the addon rights holders or resolve asset licensing. This audit records the ambiguity; it does not choose a convenient license. Obtain permission/clarification, or exclude the addon. A clean-room implementation of gameplay concepts is a distinct option only after a deliberate rights review; do not reuse questionable assets/source in that route.

MMLib is required by Corn source but is not a cloned repository here. Its [upstream repository page](https://github.com/0999312/MMLib) supplied no license determination in the bounded lookup. Do not vendor it. Optional external mods’ data schemas do not grant permission to copy their Java/assets. Build-time Minecraft/mappings artifacts and Forge MDK notices retain their own applicable terms.

### L.3 What was and was not established

Performed: inventory of all five cloned mods and template; registry/package/source-boundary inspection; authoritative FD machine/food/farming/storage/loot paths; all addon direct FD imports plus data use; later locally available generation comparison; complete JSON parsing and recipe/tag/loot indexing; registry-to-model/blockstate coverage; literal same-mod model/texture reference checks; source license/header inspection; focused primary-source Forge/Mixin research.

Not performed: Gradle dependency resolution, compilation, automated runtime tests, remap/reobf/package production, client or dedicated-server launch, profiler/benchmark, visual acceptance, or live validation of remote branch freshness. No performance improvement is measured. End later-version source, the exact MMLib implementation, installed optional provider OreDictionary membership and qualified 1.6.4 Mixin bootstrap remain named gaps. They do not prevent the independent FD milestone below.

## M. Paired foundation coding milestone

**Farmer’s Delight foundation on Forge 1.12.2 and 1.7.10 simultaneously**, entirely in this repository; no addon port yet. Shared gameplay contracts, rather than either adapter, define canonical behavior.

- Isolated builds follow the inspected ExampleMod pins. `src/` remains the 1.16.5 behavioral/resource reference; `reference/1.16.5.gradle` and `.properties` preserve its former build configuration. Production targets include only native sources plus `common`, not modern production classes.
- The foundation provides the feasible food catalog, four knife tiers (no netherite), basic materials, and Straw Bale as the initial native block registration. Crop planting/growth and other block systems wait for their paired milestones. Food definitions do not assert that every later acquisition chain is implemented.
- Nether salad, honey-dependent foods and sweet-berry foods are gated on both initial targets because their source prerequisites are absent. No substitute vanilla ecosystem is invented. The native 1.7.10 implementation omits offhand/cooldown mechanics.
- Initial crafting recipes are the four original diagonal knife recipes, water bucket + three wheat → three dough, and milk bucket + three glass bottles → three milk bottles. Both counted recipes return the native bucket. JEI/NEI read these same registered definitions, not fabricated cutting/cooking recipes.
- Ingredient identity includes item, exact metadata and NBT. Empty membership never matches. Aliases reject cycles/missing targets at freeze. Ore entries resolve live, with deterministic ordering and no wildcard species/tool metadata. Milk bucket and bottle memberships remain separate portions. Recipe IDs reject duplicates and registration freezes after integration setup.

Completion requires both compile/package results, dedicated startup, practical registry-ID parity, consistent aliases, working knives and food/container behavior, duplicate rejection, recipe enumeration in each viewer, and no 1.12-specific shared API. Client visual/interaction and equivalent gameplay checks remain distinct from headless builds and startup. Do not advance to machines while these remain unaccepted.

### M.1 Modern Regen compatibility contract and acceptance

The local Modern Regen source supports 1.6.4/1.7.10/1.8.9, not 1.12.2. Its 1.7.10 `RegenTransformer` replaces `FoodStats.onUpdate`; `RegenHooks.update` owns vanilla hunger/saturation/exhaustion fields and the food timer through `RegenAccess`. It calls native healing and adds regeneration costs directly with `FoodStats.addExhaustion`. Fast regeneration defaults to 10 ticks at full hunger/positive saturation; fallback requires hunger ≥18 and defaults to 80 ticks. Configured costs/intervals and `naturalRegeneration` remain Modern Regen-owned. `RegenAccess` exposes storage access, not an activity-exhaustion integration API.

FD therefore uses normal vanilla state, with no Modern Regen dependency, class references, reflection, shared timer or FoodStats transformer. The native Mixin on `EntityPlayer.addExhaustion` reduces positive **activity** exhaustion by at most 0.1 per player tick while Nourishment is active, without carrying unused credit across ticks or changing already accumulated exhaustion. Both vanilla and Modern Regen charge healing directly through FoodStats, outside this hook. This deliberately refines the original blanket exhaustion subtraction so healing costs cannot be refunded after the player finishes regenerating. Direct third-party FoodStats charges are also preserved. No change to Modern Regen is required by this boundary.

Comfort heals one health every 80 effect-duration ticks, ignores amplifiers, and pauses for Regeneration, positive player saturation, or eligible natural regeneration (gamerule enabled, hunger ≥18). It does not add another natural-regeneration timer or alter hunger. Below that threshold it remains supplemental healing even with Modern Regen installed. Non-player Comfort retains its original hurt/no-Regeneration rule. Food effects are applied on consumption on the owning server thread; consumption returns one bowl/bottle per survival portion and does not create containers in creative mode. Milk cures one randomly chosen milk-curable effect; cocoa limits this to harmful effects.

Required 1.7.10 configurations: FD alone; Modern Regen alone; both without effects; both + Nourishment; both + Comfort; both + both effects. Run each with `naturalRegeneration` on/off and Regeneration present/absent. Include default Modern Regen and saturation regeneration disabled, plus a changed interval/cost setting. Use hunger 0, 17, 18, 19, 20; saturation 0, 0.5, 6; health 10/20 and full health. Record starting/ending health, hunger, saturation and exhaustion, pulse ticks, activity performed, effects/durations, and consumed food/returned containers. Include fast→fallback transitions, recovery to full health, exhaustion crossing 4, repeated meals, stacked/last-item/full-inventory/creative containers, effect expiry and relog. Nourishment activity refunds must total ≤0.1/tick; healing costs and Modern Regen timer must match the no-FD control. Comfort must add no heal while natural regeneration is eligible, and must supply only its intended 80-tick pulse when ineligible. Repeat FD-only on 1.12.2; do not imply Modern Regen support there.

#### Content identity and survival gates

The Appendix 1 ledger remains the exhaustive 148-item/86-block/2-effect reference. All original IDs outside the following explicit exclusions have native implementations or the adaptations above; `wild_rice_upper` is an additional internal legacy block for the two-part wild plant.

- Both targets defer these ten source item IDs: `crimson_pantry`, `warped_pantry`, `netherite_knife`, `nether_salad`, `honey_cookie`, `honey_glazed_ham`, `honey_glazed_ham_block`, `sweet_berry_cookie`, `sweet_berry_cheesecake`, `sweet_berry_cheesecake_slice`. The corresponding four source block IDs are `crimson_pantry`, `warped_pantry`, `honey_glazed_ham_block`, `sweet_berry_cheesecake`.
- 1.7.10 additionally defers item/block IDs `beetroot_crate` and `wild_beetroots`. Existing foundation mutton-food IDs remain registered for save compatibility, but their recipes are deferred because vanilla mutton/rabbit prerequisites are absent.
- The Stove mechanic is implemented, but its source crafting recipe requires a campfire and remains deferred on both targets. Cooking Pots and Skillets can use available fire/lava heat without it; 1.12.2 also supports native magma. No fabricated campfire recipe or replacement ingredient is added.
- Basket crafting requires bamboo and remains deferred on both targets. The collector itself is fully implemented. Scaffolding's canvas recipe is deferred for the same missing prerequisite.
- Stripped-log/wood outputs are absent. All affected cutting recipes remain deferred, rather than returning the input log and allowing unlimited bark. `paper_from_tree_bark` and `organic_compost_from_tree_bark` are deferred until bark has an active acquisition recipe. `organic_compost_from_rotten_flesh` remains available.
- Native plants, dyes, fish metadata, six wood species and wooden trapdoors use explicit legacy equivalents. An equivalent input/output dye conversion that would multiply the same native item is deferred. Modern-only flowers, bamboo, kelp, scaffolding, leather horse armor, Nether vegetation and other absent inputs remain recipe-specific gates.
- Registered `fruit_salad`, `grilled_salmon`, `noodle_soup` and `stuffed_pumpkin` production is deferred on both targets because it needs berries or kelp. On 1.7.10, the beetroot/mutton-dependent `mixed_salad`, `mutton_wrap`, `roasted_mutton_chops`, `shepherds_pie`, `ratatouille`, `vegetable_soup` and `pasta_with_mutton_chop` chains are also deferred. Master Chef excludes unavailable survival meals while retaining the other original requirements.

Wild crops, knife harvesting, native animal ingredients, native crafting and cutting lead to the available pot/grill meals, feasts and storage. Seeds and rice are not added arbitrarily to grass drops. Fresh-world access and exploration of new chunks in existing worlds use the same deterministic generation; there is no retrogen. Native village heaps and the rotten-flesh compost recipe provide soil progression without tree bark.

#### Validation and remaining acceptance

Paired implementation checkpoints compiled and produced native remapped/reobfuscated JARs. Packaged server-command checks produced two cooked-rice bowl servings per target, persisted rice age and restored source water after removal, accepted vanilla mushrooms on rich soil, and persisted basket contents and feast portions. These are dedicated-server observations, not player interaction tests.

Final paired `buildAll --offline --continue` succeeded against the final production sources. Both targets compiled, packaged and remapped/reobfuscated. There are no automated test sources; both native test tasks reported NO-SOURCE.

| Final observed result | 1.7.10 | 1.12.2 |
|---|---|---|
| Production compilation and packaging | Passed; reobfuscated JAR | Passed; remapped JAR |
| Packaged dedicated-server startup/reload without viewers or Modern Regen | Ready; clean stop | Ready; clean stop |
| Optional Modern Regen 1.0.1.1 startup/reload | Ready; clean stop | No project target |
| Source items registered / reference 148 | 136; 12 explicit exclusions above | 138; 10 explicit exclusions above |
| Source blocks registered / reference 86 | 80 plus internal wild-rice upper block | 82 plus internal wild-rice upper block |
| Original crafting definitions / 109 | 88 active, 21 deferred | 94 active, 15 deferred |
| Original Cutting Board definitions / 97 | 54 active, 43 deferred | 63 active, 34 deferred |
| Original Cooking Pot definitions / 24 | 17 active, 7 deferred | 22 active, 2 deferred |
| Original smelting/smoking/blasting/campfire definitions / 28 | 23 adapted, 5 deferred | 28 adapted |
| Other-mod recipe types / 34; netherite smithing / 1 | Deferred | Deferred |
| Packaged JSON/model/texture references | No missing FD references | No missing FD references; Master Chef has 18 reachable criteria |
| Native pig/chicken constructors with the breeding Mixins | Summoned successfully | Summoned successfully |

These categories account for all 293 bundled source recipe definitions: 182 active/adapted and 111 deferred on 1.7.10; 207 active/adapted and 86 deferred on 1.12.2. Crafting totals include the six preserved foundation recipes and the Cutting Board recipe. Native vanilla grilling foods are additional to the source catalog. Smoking/blasting food routes use legacy furnaces when no native recipe already exists; placed and handheld grilling remain separate mechanics. The deferred maps and reasons are written to the native debug log for pack developers.

Fresh 1.12.2 flat-world startup with seed `1110241383260505174` reproduced the previously warning-producing seed after the population-window correction and reported no cascading-generation warning. This is one bounded generation sample, not a multi-biome acceptance result. Existing Forge update-check/unsigned-loader warnings were not suppressed; they did not prevent ready state.

Production artifacts are `versions/1.7.10/build/libs/farmersdelight-0.1.0-foundation.1+mc1.7.10-forge.jar` and `versions/1.12.2/build/libs/farmersdelight-0.1.0-foundation.1+mc1.12.2-forge.jar`. They retain the existing development version. The exact packaged JARs were installed in the disposable servers; no validation mods or test harnesses were added.

Still required: actual client loading/visual review and NEI/JEI rendering/transfer; survival crafting and harvesting; pot shift-click, containers, XP and hopper transactions; handheld cancellation, enchanted tools and dispenser behavior; feast/full-inventory accounting; sign editing and mat/piston/support behavior; safety-net bounce and combat; village/biome generation sampling; two-client simultaneous interaction and observer synchronization; save/reload under active cooking; and the full Modern Regen health/hunger/saturation/natural-regeneration/Comfort/Nourishment matrix. The milestone's gameplay acceptance remains open until those checks are performed.

## O. Four-target beta baseline and registration contract

### Scope and behavioral reference

The maintained 1.7.10 and 1.12.2 ports are co-primary behavioral references. This port preserves their completed systems and the current NEI layout and release-staging changes. The original 1.16.5 `src/` tree remains an uncompiled source/assets reference. Sections C, F, N and the Appendix ledgers cover its 148 source items, 86 blocks, two effects and 293 recipe definitions. Newer upstream additions are not silently treated as part of the 1.16.5 baseline.

`common/` owns platform-neutral food/recipe definitions, ingredient predicates, shapeless matching, effect rules, fuel rules and source identities. Native inventory/state/lifecycle implementations remain independent under `versions/<minecraft>/`; no compiled mod depends on another target JAR. The existing native growth and processing owners are reused rather than introducing a second machine implementation.

The table describes implemented source coverage, with explicit native content gates. It is not a claim that every player interaction has passed runtime acceptance.

| Feature family / reference behavior | 1.6.4 | 1.7.10 | 1.8.9 | 1.12.2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Items and food: catalog values, effects, stack limits, eating/drinking, bowl/bottle returns | Native food catalog; missing content recipes deferred | Same baseline | Same; rabbit/mutton ingredients available | Same; beetroot/rabbit/mutton available |
| Crops, planting and harvesting: cabbage, tomato, onion, two-part rice, shears and mature tomato picking | Native metadata and Forge bonemeal event | Native metadata and growable hooks | Native crop states; separate rice upper age property | Native crop states |
| Wild vegetation and acquisition | Native five vegetable patches plus rice; no beetroot | Same | Same | Adds native wild beetroot |
| Blocks and decorative variants: crates, bales, pantries, basket, mats, rug, rope, net, colored signs | Four available wood species; explicit numeric IDs | Six native wood species | Six native wood species, baked models | Same; beetroot crate |
| Cooking and processing: Cutting Board, Cooking Pot, Stove, placed/held Skillet | Complete legacy baseline; next hotbar ingredient | Same | Same | Same; offhand ingredient |
| Crafting and preparation: exact recipes, chance/Fortune/tool damage, input matching, remainders | Shared catalogs with native names/metadata and explicit gates | Same | Same | Same |
| Comfort and Nourishment | Native effects; activity exhaustion hook; optional Modern Regen | Same | Same | Native effects; no Modern Regen target |
| Entity interactions: pet feed, pig/chicken breeding/temptation, knife loot, rotten tomatoes, Backstabbing | Existing native animals and combat | Same | Adds native rabbit hide; native rabbit-stew effects | Later native mobs where implemented |
| World generation | Bounded native population window and biome conditions; native village heaps | Same | Same | Same |
| Loot and trades | Additive dungeon/mineshaft pools; native farmer purchases | Same | Additive native pools; native farmer career/level purchases | Native loot-table hooks and farmer careers |
| GUIs and inventories | Original pot artwork/slots, server container, progress sync, shift-click, sided inventories | Same | Same | Same plus item-handler capabilities |
| Rendering, particles and sounds | Prepared legacy models/TESRs, icon sprites, native sound-load registration, potion atlas icons | Prepared legacy models/TESRs | Native states, 1.8 model schema, pane variants and TESRs | Native states/models/TESRs |
| Automation and interoperability | Pot sided slot rules, basket cooldown/redstone, dispenser tools, Forge tool registry and exact Ore Dictionary memberships | Same | Same, native Forge APIs | Same plus native capabilities |
| Recipe viewers and transfer | Optional native NEI categories and overlay handlers | Optional NEI, including current page improvements | Optional native NEI categories/overlay handlers | Optional JEI categories/transfer |
| Progression | Server-persisted criteria, numeric native achievements and once-only XP rewards | Native achievements | Native achievements | Native advancements |

### Content gates and adaptations

No missing mobs, mutton, beetroot, bamboo, campfires, honey, sweet berries, Nether vegetation, netherite, bells or general waterlogging are backported for recipe availability. Unsupported definitions stay in the deferred catalogs with reasons. Stove and Basket mechanics exist, while their campfire/bamboo crafting prerequisites remain deferred. Stripped-log outputs are absent; log cutting must not turn unchanged logs into an unlimited bark source. The rotten-flesh compost route remains available.

The 1.6.4 target additionally rejects later fish/flower/wood metadata instead of registering invalid variants. Its pantries use oak, spruce, birch and jungle. Source village templates use available jungle wood for unavailable later wood palettes, within structure adaptation only; this does not create global Ore Dictionary equivalence or make unavailable recipes active. Later mob-dependent food IDs retained by the established catalog do not imply obtainable recipes.

Source CraftTweaker adapters and the 34 Botany Pots/Create/Immersive Engineering recipe definitions remain deferred on all legacy targets. The native viewers do not list deferred processing definitions as active recipes. NEI is compile-only and is never bundled or required for a server. Native NEI distribution/dependency compatibility and actual transfer still require a functioning client.

Comfort remains supplemental; Nourishment intercepts player activity exhaustion and does not refund direct natural-regeneration charges. Modern Regen remains the optional owner of its own regeneration, hunger state and costs. A startup check with Modern Regen is distinct from player effect/health/hunger acceptance.

### Native compatibility boundaries

- **1.6.4:** Unimined 1.4.1 / Forge 9.11.1.1345, JDK 21 build process, JDK 17 `--release 7` compiler, Java 8 game runtime. Stock Forge's ASM 4.1 remains unchanged. Explicit vanilla identities use actual native block/item fields and metadata. Numeric registration, older FML packets/connections/player/crafting callbacks, native bonemeal, primitive block IDs, older tile NBT packets and inventory hooks remain local to this target. Portable workstation harvests preserve tile data before the old removal callback destroys it.
- **1.8.9:** Unimined 1.4.1 / Forge 11.15.1.2318, Java 8 distribution/runtime. Native `BlockPos`, block states, property serialization, inventory/container callbacks and item models are used. The rice upper is a native bush with its own 0–3 age property because 1.8's `BlockCrops` hard-codes a different property. Rope multipart assets are expanded to native pane variants. Native farmer careers use a narrow Mixin at trade-list population because that Forge version exposes no public trade-extension method.
- **Mixin bootstrap:** The two new targets embed pinned SpongePowered Mixin 0.7.11 with its relocated ASM, whose classes are Java 5/6 compatible. Its service classes load from the parent launch loader before transformations; no launcher or stock Forge library is replaced. 1.6.4 includes Java-7-compatible Log4j 2.12.4 for Mixin. Its already generated refmap preserves FML runtime class names through Unimined packaging, rather than being remapped a second time to the obsolete `net.minecraft.src` class layer. Development runs disable the production SRG refmap while running mapped classes. Existing UniMixins and MixinBooter setups on the maintained targets are preserved.
- **Threading and networking:** Native world/container threads own inventory, growth, processing, drops and effects. The 1.6 packet is a bounded server-to-client recipe-settings snapshot; client-originated snapshots are rejected, and application is queued to the client tick. The 1.8 snapshot is scheduled on the client thread. Native inventory, tile, sign, container and entity packets carry gameplay state. The 1.6.4 achievement identities are registered in pre-initialization before local statistics are loaded; recipe-dependent criteria are resolved after optional integrations.
- **Performance:** Input changes invalidate pot recipe selection; cutting inputs are indexed and grilling selections are cached per slot. Idle machines keep their existing small constant-time checks. Heat uses native object identities, world generation is clipped to its population window, and prepared models/textures are resolved on initialization/resource reload rather than reparsed per frame. These are architectural/source properties, not measured frame-rate or server-performance claims.

### Registration and save contract

The [1.6.4 registry manifest](versions/1.6.4/src/main/resources/farmersdelight/registry164.json) is the authoritative frozen default allocation, keyed by logical registry name, not constructor order. Its block slots start at 3000, separate item slots at 5000, and achievement offsets at 8000 (effective native stat IDs are `5242880 + offset`). ItemBlock numeric IDs equal their block IDs. FML 1.6.4 requires ItemBlocks to track blocks; hidden crop/sign forms register under `<block_name>_technical` to avoid colliding with separately registered seeds/sign items, and remain outside the creative tab. Rope, Skillet and Tatami therefore use their native ItemBlock slots; reserved gaps are intentional. The `block`, `item` and `achievement` configuration categories preserve named overrides; clients and server must agree. Startup rejects occupied, duplicate and out-of-range slots. Future additions must receive new explicit entries without renumbering existing names. Existing-world numeric changes require a deliberate save migration.

1.8.9 uses stable `farmersdelight:<name>` identities matching the maintained legacy target where content exists. The following metadata contracts apply to the new targets:

| State | Persisted representation |
|---|---|
| Cabbage/tomato/onion | Age 0–7 |
| Rice lower | Water metadata remains separate; tile `Age` 0–3 |
| Rice upper / mushroom colony | Age 0–3 |
| Rich farmland / compost | Moisture / composting stage 0–7 |
| Cutting Board / serving block | Four horizontal directions |
| Cooking Pot | Low two facing bits; support bits select normal, tray or handle |
| Stove / Skillet | Low two facing bits; mask `4` indicates lit |
| Pantry / Basket | Native facing metadata; Basket supports six directions |
| Tatami / full mat | Tatami: native six-face value in low three bits, paired mask `8`. Full mat: horizontal facing in low two bits, head mask `4`; placement/removal is owned by the server |
| Standing / wall canvas signs | Rotation 0–15 / native horizontal face; persisted text/color use native tile packets |
| Shared meals | Direction in metadata; tile `Servings` 0–4, so partial meals cannot become full ones on reload |

Tile identities remain `farmersdelight:cutting_board`, `cooking_pot`, `stove`, `skillet`, `rice`, `serving`, `pantry`, `basket`, and `canvas_sign`. Inventory contents, cooking progress/recipe identity/container, stored meals, recipe XP counts, skillet reservation/payment state, basket cooldown, feast portions and sign text/color remain serialized by their authoritative native owner. Rice removal recognizes its source-water replacement as a successful player harvest, preserving the native drop callback instead of treating the changed chunk replacement as a failed removal. No cross-Minecraft world conversion is promised.

### Beta validation and acceptance

**2026-10-20 implementation checkpoint:** all four targets compile, produce native production archives and reach dedicated-server ready state. This is a beta candidate implementation; the player acceptance gate below remains open. No builds were published.

A clean four-target `clean testAll buildAll` checkpoint passed. After the final rice harvest return correction, `testAll buildAll` recompiled the affected native sources and passed again. Every native test task reported `NO-SOURCE`; there is no automated gameplay suite. Compilation, native remap/reobfuscation, packaging and runtime evidence are separate:

| Target | Final compilation | Native automated tests | Final production packaging | Final packaged dedicated server | Development client initialization |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.6.4 / Forge 9.11.1.1345 | Passed; maximum class major 51 | NO-SOURCE | Unimined remap and release verification passed | Ready, existing world reopened, saved and stopped; Modern Regen absent/present | Mod loading and native icon/texture loading passed after achievement-lifecycle correction |
| 1.7.10 / Forge 10.13.4.1614 | Passed; maximum class major 52 | NO-SOURCE | Native reobfuscation and release verification passed | Ready, existing world reopened, saved and stopped; UniMixins and Modern Regen present | Mod loading and atlas initialization passed |
| 1.8.9 / Forge 11.15.1.2318 | Passed; maximum class major 52 | NO-SOURCE | Unimined remap and release verification passed | Ready, saved machine state reopened, saved and stopped; Modern Regen absent/present | Mod loading and model/texture atlas initialization passed |
| 1.12.2 / Forge 14.23.5.2859 | Passed; maximum class major 52 | NO-SOURCE | Unimined remap and release verification passed | Ready, existing world reopened, saved and stopped; MixinBooter present | Mod loading, native model atlas and JEI initialization passed |

All server runs used Java 8 and unchanged native Forge libraries. The tested server copies matched the staged distribution SHA-256 hashes. Archive inspection found exactly four staged distributions, correct mod metadata/version, no duplicate ZIP entries, valid referenced Farmer's Delight models/textures, required licenses and the intended class-version limits. The final native harvest overrides are present in all four production archives. The new targets boot without NEI installed. Client initialization was performed with the native development tasks, not a packaged launcher profile; no Farmer's Delight missing texture/model errors remained in the corrected startup logs. The final rice-removal return correction was then compiled and checked on the packaged servers; actual player harvesting remains an acceptance item.

Default native catalogs observed at startup (active / deferred):

| Catalog | 1.6.4 | 1.7.10 | 1.8.9 | 1.12.2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cooking Pot | 17 / 7 | 17 / 7 | 19 / 5 | 22 / 2 |
| Cutting Board | 42 / 55 | 54 / 43 | 61 / 36 | 63 / 34 |
| Additional source crafting, excluding the shared foundation recipes | 77 / 25 | 81 / 21 | 83 / 19 | 87 / 15 |

The differences reflect native wood, flowers, fish, mobs and later content gates, not empty workstation implementations. Optional ingredient providers and configuration can change catalog availability.

**Observed 1.8.9 machine/persistence smoke:** three rice and three bowls produced exactly three cooked-rice servings. A save at `CookTime=91`, with one completed serving and two remaining inputs/containers, resumed after restart and finished with three servings, no remaining input bowls and three recorded recipe completions. Subsequent final-artifact restarts preserved those three servings, a basket's eleven rice, a feast's two remaining portions and the dispenser knife's damage. A powered native dispenser processed one porkchop into two bacon, emptied the board and charged one knife durability. Rice lower age 3 persisted across restart; native destruction restored source water. Administrative replacement commands have different semantics from destruction and are not used as evidence of player harvesting.

**Remaining release gate and known limitations:** no known remaining critical startup or packaging defect was found in the checks above. This does not certify every player interaction. Still required are survival acquisition/crafting and player harvesting; food/container/Comfort/Nourishment behavior with vanilla hunger and the full Modern Regen health/hunger/saturation/regeneration matrix; visual review of all world/inventory states, particles, signs and GUIs at multiple scales; native 1.6.4/1.8.9 NEI distribution/dependency loading, recipe pages/usages and actual transfer; pot shift-click, full-inventory/remainder/XP handling and hopper transactions; 1.6.4 processing/save-reload with players; disconnects during open containers; two-client simultaneous interaction/observer synchronization; biome/village generation samples; and combat, skillet cancellation, mats/pistons and safety-net behavior. Startup resource loading is not visual or GUI acceptance. No performance benchmark or long-running server soak was performed.

The original unavailable-content and integration gates above remain intentional limitations. Do not describe this milestone as unrestricted 1.16.5 parity or approved public-beta release readiness until the player acceptance gate is completed. Candidate artifacts are staged as `build/release/farmersdelight-0.1.0+mc<minecraft>.jar`; installation uses one matching target JAR only.

## Appendices: source ledgers

The appendices below are part of this single audit document. They are static source evidence and implementation checklists. A listed recipe output or Java reference is **not** a proven reachable acquisition path; optional conditions, unsupported vanilla inputs, source defects and manual interactions must be applied as explained above. No generated source/test infrastructure is required to use these ledgers.

### Appendix 2. Every checked-out recipe ID and exact resource payload

These are compact representations of the existing resources, not generated runtime configuration. All pattern, duplicate ingredient, count, chance, tool, NBT, container, time, XP, temperature, conditional and optional-machine fields are retained. Interpret defaults through C/D/G; in particular Brewin `cookingtime` is not read by its current serializer. IDs are unique within each mod’s included resource roots. Foreign compatibility serializers are not native crafting recipes.

### Appendix 3. Every tag definition and tag reference

Block/entity/integration tags do not become ingredient OreDictionary entries. Nested tags keep typed membership. The JSON ledger preserves optional IDs and `replace`. External references with no local definition require the base game/Forge/optional provider, or deliberate FD-owned legacy membership. Java helper declarations are listed separately so source-only concepts are not missed.

## P. Native addon integration (2026-10-23)

The requested addon targets are Forge 1.7.10 and 1.12.2. The original addon `src/` trees remain references; isolated target source and independent distribution builds live under each addon's `versions/` directories. Missing modern items and dependencies are reserved for future UAB support: preserve exact prerequisites and defer recipes, without invented substitute ingredients or environmental acquisition paths.

Farmer's Delight Legacy 1.0.2 exposes `SourceRecipes.addCatalog(namespace, owner, resource)` and target-native `SourceTags.addCatalog(owner, resource)`. Addons call both during FML initialization. FD freezes the catalogs during post-initialization and its existing native processors compile recipes after all addon initialization. Namespaced recipe collisions and replacing tags fail loudly. Addons do not package FD classes. `FoodDefinition.effects` optionally adds effects through the existing native food consumption behavior, preserving the original single-effect fields.

| Addon feature | 1.7.10 | 1.12.2 | Implementation boundary |
|---|---|---|---|
| Corn: 14 foods, edible plantable kernels, 3 block items | Native | Native | FD nutrition, effects and container handling; native registration |
| Corn: eight-stage lower and upper stalk | Metadata | Block state | Upper begins after lower age 4; independent aging, bonemeal and crop drops |
| Corn: wild plants and shears acquisition | Native | Native | Configured temperate overworld clusters, bounded to the current chunk |
| Corn: crafting, pot, cutting and grilling | FD processors | FD processors | Original 39-resource catalog and additive tags; unavailable prerequisites defer |
| Corn: sweet-berry stuffing/pumpkin recipes | Deferred | Deferred | Await original sweet berries through a future provider such as UAB |
| Corn: modern Create / IE serializers | Deferred | Deferred | No legacy adapters; source recipes retained |
| Corn: modern chest tables / composter / TFC metadata | Unavailable | Unavailable | No replacement chests, composting or nutrition simulation invented |
| Ocean: 22 registered items, foods and Guardian Soup feast | Native | Native | Original food/effect contracts and four persistent servings |
| Ocean: squid knife drops and tentacle processing | Native | Native | Native death/drop callbacks and FD processors |
| Ocean: guardian / elder guardian acquisition | Deferred | Native | No replacement guardian mobs on 1.7.10 |
| Ocean: kelp, seagrass, honey and pickle chains | Deferred | Deferred | Original prerequisites retained; dependent stuffed-cod recipe deferred |
| End: 53 registered items, seven blocks and four knives | Native | Native | Original nutrition, effects, materials, servings and repair identities |
| End: dragon knife drops, stove processing and feasts | Native | Native | Native mob drops, six-slot stove, heat integration and persistent servings |
| End: native shulkers, endermites, chorus and outer-island succulent generation | Deferred | Native | No replacement mobs, chorus acquisition or outer islands on 1.7.10 |
| End: campfire stove recipe and Farmer's Respite brewing | Deferred | Deferred | Missing original content/dependency; no substitute recipes |
| Brewin: 33 registered items, seven blocks and three status effects | Native | Native | Source-derived food contract; native potion allocation/registry |
| Brewin: eight-slot keg, duration, temperature, serving, XP and persistence | Native | Native | Actual server-side processing; distinct ingredient/liquid/buffer/container/output slots |
| Brewin: automation, portable internal batches and native screens | Native | Native | Top inserts ingredient/liquid; other sides serve/extract output; buffer is locked |
| Brewin: cheese aging, wheel/pizza portions and item-mat display | Native | Native | Four portions; partial break drops preserve remaining quantity; one persistent mat item |
| Brewin: honeycomb keg/cheese and dependent survival paths | Deferred | Deferred | Await real honeycomb through future UAB; creative machines remain functional |
| Brewin: tankard / tankard-dependent fermentation recipes | Deferred | Native | Tankard's original iron nugget is unavailable on 1.7.10 |
| Brewin: kelp, honey, berries, pickle, turtle-egg and tea recipes | Deferred | Deferred | Exact unavailable inputs and dependent alcohol chains remain deferred |
| Fermentation recipe viewer and transfer | Optional NEI | Optional JEI | Uses actual registered recipes, liquid slot and serving-container input; excludes the locked buffer |
| Modern Regen | Optional | Optional | Addons do not own its regeneration timers or healing logic |

The available MMLib 1.16.5 source branch contains food behavior but omits the `HighCropBlock` and edible-seed classes required by this Corn checkout. Later MMLib branches confirm the independent-half growth design; they do not certify the exact missing 1.1.1 binary. The native crop is an adaptation with this explicit dependency-evidence limit. MMLib implementation code is not vendored.


`AddonRecipes.register(namespace, roots, extraTypes...)` declares real native acquisition roots and processors. The startup fixed point rejects addon-owned ingredient chains without acquisition. `AddonRecipes.processing(namespace, input, output)` declares genuine non-recipe conversions such as unripe-to-ripe cheese aging; it does not fabricate prerequisites. Catalogs and tags freeze after all addon initialization and before the native processors register. Cutting tools may be exact item/tag ingredients, including End's Nether Star tool, as well as existing knife/shears/tool-class predicates.

The native integration also permits qualified serving items, explicit feast leftovers, addon knife subclasses, addon stove heat, additional named food effects, food-effect tooltips, and 1.7.10 metadata model sets. Rendering remains client-only. No addon bundles the core classes. The existing rice water block exposes native liquid level zero on 1.8.9 and 1.12.2 so vanilla flow inspection can coexist with its tile-backed age and restored water behavior.

Brewin preserves actual fermentation rather than crafting substitutes. The port accepts both upstream duration field spellings, enforces all five temperature ranges, permits liquid automation, validates buffer/container identity and capacity, preserves placed progress and stored XP, and avoids resetting partial wheel portions by replacement. Tipsy intercepts only vanilla food-healing calls, so Sweet Heart cannot revive a zero-amount food heal and Modern Regen retains its own ownership. Satisfaction uses a server-safe 1.7.10 hunger update. Bad Omen and waterlogging have no native counterpart on either target; 1.7.10 additionally omits unavailable Glowing/Luck/Levitation effects. Absent recipe prerequisites continue to gate their associated content.

Ocean's checked-in metadata and license text disagree (MIT declaration versus LGPL boilerplate). The local port retains the source notices; publication rights need resolution before distributing that addon publicly. This initiative does not publish releases.
